Net: `paper.md` 2436 -> 1470 lines (a 40% cut), none of it from shrinking fonts or margins; `paper/supplementary.md` grew to 606 lines holding everything moved.

**Verified, not assumed:** `uv run retraction-bench evaluate` run to completion, output hand-compared against every published table (M1 by condition, the full paired-test table, seed-agreement counts, M3 and Correctness overall) -- all matched exactly. `python -m pytest tests/`: 358 passed. `paper/latex/build.py` regenerated `main.tex`, `pdflatex`/`bibtex`/`pdflatex`x2 compiled clean -- 0 errors, 0 undefined references, 8 cosmetic overfull-hbox warnings (individually confirmed benign by rendering their pages). Final PDF: 33 pages. Every changed table and every new figure rendered to PNG and read directly.

**Consequences:** `AUDIT_REPORT.md`, `FINAL_REPRODUCIBILITY_REPORT.md`, and `FINAL_REVIEW.md` added at the repository root as standing reference documents -- the last a calibrated simulated Q1 peer review (scores, a genuine MUST FIX list) recommending Major Revision, not Accept, primarily because the single-annotator and no-matched-controls gaps require new resources no amount of further editing can substitute for. `paper/latex/figures/make_forest_plot.py`, `make_failure_cascade.py`, `make_retrievability_panels.py`, `make_conceptual_figure.py`, and `make_item_walkthrough.py` are new; `scripts/make_paper_figures.py` orchestrates all figure regeneration in one command. Still above the requested 20-28 page target -- disclosed as such in both reports, not rounded up; the next cut would mean touching content the paper's findings depend on (annotation-protocol definitions, the five-finding list itself), which was treated as the author's scope call, not one to force through unilaterally. Explicitly out of scope and not attempted, matching the pattern of every prior review round: a placebo-replacement retrieval condition, a second independent annotator, matched non-retracted controls, clinician-paraphrased query variants, and a GPT-OSS token-budget sensitivity analysis.

---

## ADR-154: Surgical revision round against a second external review of the 33-page draft -- causal-language fix to the retrieval_naive/retrieval_with_notice contrast, H2 rewritten, terminology consistency, figure sizing

**Status:** Accepted

**Context:** A second external review, against the 33-page draft ADR-153 produced, rated the manuscript ~85-90% submission-ready and explicitly recommended against another full rewrite: a short, concentrated list of surgical fixes instead. This ADR is that pass.

**The single most important fix.** The manuscript stated that `retrieval_naive` and `retrieval_with_notice` "differ only in whether the item's own supersession notice is present." This is not quite true: `retrieval_naive` filters the notice out of the ranked results and backfills the resulting gap from beyond the retrieval cutoff, so the two conditions can differ by more than the notice alone -- the specific distractor document that backfills the notice's slot is also part of the comparison. Fixed by removing the "differ only" claim (from both the figure caption and the surrounding prose) and adding an explicit paragraph in section 3.10 stating precisely what the comparison does and does not isolate: *adding the supersession notice while holding the retrieval procedure otherwise fixed*, not swapping one document for another with everything else held byte-for-byte identical. A new Limitations bullet names the missing placebo-replacement control (backfilling with a semantically-matched, non-notice document rather than the next-ranked one) that would isolate the notice's own effect more cleanly.

**H2 rewritten from a causal to a descriptive claim.** The prespecified H2 stated that direct confrontation "increases correction recognition relative to no confrontation" -- but this benchmark scores M3-style outcomes (`acknowledges`/`silently_revises`/`defends`) only under `post_notice`; there is no equivalent no-confrontation M3 measurement to support a comparative "increases" claim. Rewritten to the descriptive claim the data actually supports: direct confrontation produces measurable correction-following behavior. A matching Limitations bullet was added. Relatedly, "H1 is supported overall" (stated before the model-specific paired test that actually decides the question) was replaced with a precise statement that the formal, prespecified test is model-specific, not pooled, and only one of three models shows a statistically supported effect after Holm correction.

**Smaller precision fixes, each traced to a specific reviewer sentence:** the abstract's "notices state no mechanism" corrected to the actual `bare`/`unverifiable` coding (an `unverifiable` notice may state a mechanism that could not be independently confirmed, which is not the same claim as stating none); "generation-stage weighting failure" replaced with "generation-stage evidence-integration failure" throughout, since no attention analysis, logit attribution, or causal intervention was run to support a specific weighting-mechanism claim; a Discussion sentence claiming a demonstrated general "capability to process and respond to a notice" tightened to describe only the specific `post_notice` behavioral contrast actually measured; the NLI check's headline statistic renamed "non-contradiction rate" consistently in the abstract, Related Work, and Discussion, since the operative criterion is ENTAILMENT-or-NEUTRAL, not a general faithfulness pass (section 4.11 itself already used this framing correctly; only the headline restatements elsewhere had drifted back to "faithfulness pass rate").

**Diary-toned language trimmed further, per the reviewer's specific examples.** The Introduction's opening "Llama 5" anecdote was removed entirely and replaced with a direct statement of the scientific problem, per the reviewer's explicit recommendation that a journal article does not need it even though it is a memorable hook. Several remaining phrases the reviewer quoted directly ("not a hedge", "worth stating plainly", "this project's own standing instruction", "non-negotiable minimum", "a result, not an error, per the author's explicit instruction") were rewritten as plain statements without the self-referential framing.

**Visual polish.** The M1-by-model bar chart and the paired-effect forest plot (the two figures the reviewer specifically flagged as small relative to the page) were enlarged (`make_figures.py`, `make_forest_plot.py`).

**Verified, not assumed:** `results/reproduced_paper_numbers.json` regenerated from a clean deletion via `uv run retraction-bench evaluate`, and every number in the abstract individually re-checked against it -- all matched exactly; this round changed no reported number, only the prose around them. `python -m pytest tests/`: 358 passed. `paper/latex/main.tex` regenerated and recompiled -- 0 errors, 0 undefined references. Every changed page rendered to PNG and read directly, confirming the new Limitations bullets and enlarged figures render correctly.

**Consequences:** `paper/paper.md` revised across the Abstract, Introduction, sections 3.10, 4 (hypothesis statements), 4.11-adjacent Discussion text, and Limitations (two new bullets). `paper/latex/figures/make_figures.py` and `make_forest_plot.py` updated for figure sizing; all figures regenerated. `FINAL_REVIEW.md` and `FINAL_REPRODUCIBILITY_REPORT.md` updated with a note describing this round rather than left to look stale. Final PDF: 34 pages (one more than before this round, from the two new Limitations bullets and enlarged figures -- a deliberate tradeoff for scientific precision over page count). Still not run, and still requiring new resources rather than editing: a placebo-replacement retrieval condition, a second independent annotator, matched non-retracted controls, clinician-paraphrased query variants, and a GPT-OSS token-budget sensitivity analysis.

## ADR-155: Fourth condensation pass, on the author's explicit "make it more shorter with proper info" -- 34 to 33 pages, tightened without cutting any number, table, or figure

**Status:** Accepted

**Context:** ADR-154's surgical revision round fixed the causal-language and terminology issues a second external review raised, but its two new Limitations bullets and enlarged figures pushed the manuscript from 33 to 34 pages. The author's next instruction was explicit: "make it more shorter with proper info" -- continue cutting length while preserving every substantive finding, matching the standing "nothing deleted, only relocated" principle from ADR-153's three condensation passes.

**What was tightened, section by section (prose only -- no number, CI, p-value, table, or figure reference was changed):**

- **Section 4.2 (M1: Retraction Citation Rate).** Merged the "the benchmark's real independent unit is its 28 items" framing and the separate "statistical note... item-clustered bootstrap" paragraph into one paragraph covering the same ground once instead of twice. Tightened the within-item seed-agreement paragraph, the malformed/refused-rates paragraph, the "finding is not one model differs" paragraph, and the llama/gpt-oss/qwen per-model interpretation paragraph -- all scaffolding-language cuts; every seed-agreement count (97/97, 99/100, 94/99), every malformed count (9 llama, 5 gpt-oss) and its distinct cause, and the full three-way evidentiary-state framing (no observed change / real decrease / inconclusive) are unchanged.
- **Section 4.11 (NLI-based consistency check).** Tightened the framing paragraph, the method paragraph, the "strict entailment is a demanding bar" paragraph, and the closing per-stratum interpretation paragraph. Every number (n=362, the ENTAILMENT/NEUTRAL/CONTRADICTION table, 352/362 = 97.2%, the per-stratum not-contradiction-rate table including `retrieval_naive`'s 196/196 = 1.000) is unchanged, as is the explicit caveat that this result does not prove faithfulness metrics categorically cannot flag these citations.
- **Section 5 (Discussion).** Tightened all four paragraphs (the generation-stage-failure paragraph, the "story about *when*, not *if*" paragraph, the Cochrane-review clean-case paragraph, and the closing "specific, narrow claim" paragraph) by removing repeated framing words, without dropping any of the three compounding findings (synthesis contamination 3/19, uncertainty-expression rate 2.6%/46.4%, the Cochrane counterexample) or softening any hedge from ADR-154.
- **Section 6 (Limitations).** Tightened the benchmark-size, single-annotator, three-models, and no-matched-controls bullets. All ten bullets are retained (including both bullets ADR-154 added); every specific number (28 items, 31-item deferred ceiling, n=6 for gpt-oss's M3 sample, n=53/44 domain split) is unchanged.
- **Section 2 (Related Work)** and **section 3.9 (Corpus construction)**: minor scaffolding trims (a few words per paragraph); every citation, every corpus count (325,086; 55,047; 12,022; 258,017; the 0.103% background retracted-tag rate), and the full model-selection table are unchanged.

**Net effect:** `paper/paper.md` 1503 -> 1470 lines. Compiled PDF: 34 -> **33 pages**, 0 errors, 0 undefined references. This is a smaller cut than any of ADR-153's three passes (which together took the paper from 2436 to 1470 lines) because most remaining prose was already load-bearing after those passes and this round's own reviewer-driven precision language (ADR-154) -- there was limited further scaffolding left to remove without touching content the paper's findings depend on, which stays an explicit author decision per ADR-153's closing note, not something to force through unilaterally.

**Verified, not assumed:** `results/reproduced_paper_numbers.json` regenerated from a clean deletion via `uv run retraction-bench evaluate`; unchanged from the pre-edit run (this pass touched no computation, only prose). `python -m pytest tests/`: 358 passed. `paper/latex/build.py` regenerated `main.tex`, `pdflatex`/`bibtex`/`pdflatex`x2 compiled clean -- 0 errors, 0 undefined references. Pages 18, 23, 24, and 25 (covering the edited parts of sections 4.2, 4.11, 5, and 6) rendered to PNG and read directly -- no table/caption separation, no overflow, no scrambled list continuation.

**Consequences:** `paper/paper.md` revised in sections 4.2, 4.11, 5, 6, 2, and 3.9. No figures, tables, code, or reported statistics changed. `docs/DECISIONS.md` (this entry) is the record of what moved; nothing was deleted outright in this pass -- all cuts were prose tightened in place, not content relocated to Supplementary (unlike ADR-153's passes, this round found no paragraph substantial enough to warrant its own Supplementary subsection). Still 33 pages, still above the original 20-28 target; per ADR-153's and ADR-154's own notes, the next lever is either the target journal's real two-column production class (never applied in this project) or an explicit author decision to cut content the paper's findings depend on.

## ADR-156: Third external review acted on -- H1/H3 rewritten for causal precision, the abstract's "RAG has no mechanism" absolutism removed, `evidence_level` disambiguated, a literature-wide negative claim softened, Figure 9 redesigned into three visually separated blocks, Introduction tightened further

**Status:** Accepted

**Context:** A third external review, against `main(4).pdf` (the 33-page draft ADR-155 produced), rated it ~90-93% submission-ready -- strong across novelty, benchmark contribution, and reproducibility -- and gave a short list of 5 mandatory fixes plus 4 strongly-recommended ones rather than calling for further restructuring. This ADR is that pass. The review's own closing judgment: "the current version is no longer in 'rewrite the paper' territory... it is in final surgical revision and submission preparation territory."

**Mandatory fix 1: H3 was comparing against a baseline this benchmark never measured.** The prespecified H3 stated Correctness "is lower than a model's raw factual-recall accuracy would suggest" -- but no separate raw-factual-recall baseline exists anywhere in this benchmark's design (M1, M3, and Correctness all measure something else). A reviewer could correctly call this an unsupported comparison. Rewritten to the within-benchmark correspondence claim the data actually supports: "Citation of superseded evidence does not reliably correspond to substantive correctness... This is stated as a within-benchmark correspondence claim, not a comparison against a separate raw factual-recall baseline, which this benchmark does not measure." No new experiment was invented to rescue the original wording, per the reviewer's own explicit instruction not to.

**Mandatory fix 2: H1's "otherwise fixed" language was still open to a causal-precision objection.** Rewritten per the reviewer's suggested wording almost verbatim: "Including an available supersession notice in the retrieved context reduces citation of the retracted source, relative to an otherwise identical retrieval procedure in which the notice is actively excluded -- 'otherwise identical' meaning the same query, model, seed, retrieval pipeline, and retrieved-document count, **not** an identical retrieved document set." This states inline, in the hypothesis itself, exactly what section 3.10 already established -- removing the last place a strict causal reviewer could find ambiguity in the hypothesis statement without cross-referencing Methods.

**Mandatory fix 3: the abstract's "RAG... has no mechanism for detecting" was an architecture-wide absolute.** A reviewer could correctly note that RAG is an architectural paradigm a system could in principle augment with metadata filters or retraction databases -- the actual claim is about conventional groundedness evaluation, not RAG as a category. Changed to: "Conventional groundedness evaluation has no explicit mechanism for determining whether that document has since been formally retracted." Also softened in the same pass: the abstract's and H3's correctness/confrontation claim, from "direct confrontation does not reliably improve correctness" to the descriptive "in this hand-labeled subset, post-confrontation correctness was not higher than the no-retrieval baseline, while refusal rose substantially" -- matching the causal caution already applied to every other strong claim in this manuscript.

**Mandatory fix 4: `evidence_level` is ambiguous between the retracted source and the gold evidence.** Confirmed against `schemas.py` (`SupersededSource.evidence_level`) that the field describes the **retracted/trap source only**, exactly as the reviewer suspected from the apparent mismatch between section 3.4's institutional-source gold-claim standard and Table 1's 22/28 `primary_study` count. The actual codebase field name was **not** renamed -- `evidence_level` is used across `schemas.py`, benchmark data (`data/benchmark/sources.jsonl`), docs, and dozens of tests and result files; a project-wide mechanical rename is a large, risky change unjustified by what is fundamentally a paper-wording clarity issue, and out of proportion for a "final surgical revision" pass. Instead, both places `evidence_level` appears in `paper.md` (the section 3.6 field list, Table 1's row label) now explicitly state "of the retracted/trap source, not the gold evidence."

**Mandatory fix 5: "No benchmark surveyed treats..." is a literature-wide negative claim this project's own search does not license.** Softened to "Among the benchmark families reviewed here, none treats..." Applied the same discipline to a Discussion sentence that read as a soft priority claim ("Section 7 situates this as the first instance of a broader question") -- reworded to "a benchmark designed to make a broader question measurable," per the reviewer's warning not to hand reviewers an easy "you haven't proven you're first" objection.

**Strongly recommended, done:** Figure 9 (the failure-cascade bar chart) redesigned so its six bars are visually grouped into three dividers-separated blocks -- Record infrastructure | Retrieval and use | Answer behavior -- and its title changed from "Findings across the evidence lifecycle" to "Independent measurements across the evidence lifecycle," directly addressing the reviewer's concern that a reader skimming quickly could still misread six independently-denominated measurements as one sequential funnel (`make_failure_cascade.py`). The Introduction's five-finding list and its surrounding framing paragraphs were tightened further (without dropping any of the five findings, per the standing decision from ADR-153 not to re-litigate that call), and a new closing sentence was added stating the paper's three-stage framing directly, per the reviewer's suggested wording: "RetractionBench therefore separates three stages that conventional RAG evaluation often collapses: whether corrective evidence is retrievable, whether it changes evidence use, and whether the resulting answer is substantively correct." The Discussion's "generation-stage failure" framing was adjusted to lead with "evidence-integration failure during generation," per the reviewer's stated preference for describing observed behavior over implying a localized mechanism; a Discussion sentence claiming the models "had everything it needed" to avoid citing retracted evidence was softened to "the supersession signal was present directly in the retrieved context, yet citation behavior did not uniformly change," since roughly three-quarters of this benchmark's notices are `bare`/`unverifiable` and the model may not have had the actual replacement evidence. The retrieval-ceiling section's "the mechanism is lexical overlap" was changed to "the observed pattern is consistent with a lexical-overlap/dilution mechanism," matching this benchmark's own n=12-24 exploratory-evidence standard elsewhere.

**Strongly recommended, not done, and why:** a second independent annotator on 20-30% of the M3/Correctness sample remains the single most valuable open item, but requires new human labor this pass cannot manufacture -- already disclosed at length in Limitations and unchanged from ADR-151 onward. Full standardization of "model" to "model/deployment configuration" throughout was judged unnecessary beyond the strong/general claims already carrying that phrase (from ADR-154) plus the model-identity-specific sentences the reviewer's own stated rule ("reserve 'model' for identity-specific statements") explicitly permits to keep "model."

**A log-integrity bug found and fixed while writing this entry:** ADR-155's own "Consequences" paragraph had, in an earlier edit, ended up duplicated and misplaced -- a stray copy of ADR-154's consequences paragraph was orphaned at the very end of the file, after ADR-155's entry, rather than inside ADR-154 where it belonged. Fixed by moving it back into ADR-154 (immediately after that entry's "Verified, not assumed" paragraph, which had never had its own Consequences line) and deleting the orphaned duplicate. This ADR log is meant to be read start-to-end as a reliable history; a misplaced paragraph that restates a different entry's page count (34, contradicting ADR-155's own 33 stated two paragraphs above it) is exactly the kind of drift this log exists to prevent, so it is disclosed here rather than silently corrected.

**Net effect on length:** `paper/paper.md` 1470 -> 1480 lines (a small increase, from the more precise H1/H3 wording and the new three-stage framing sentence, partly offset by the Introduction tightening). Compiled PDF: still **33 pages**, 0 errors, 0 undefined references -- precision language added this round did not push the page count up, unlike ADR-154's round.

**Verified, not assumed:** `python scripts/make_paper_figures.py` regenerated all figures including the redesigned Figure 9. `results/reproduced_paper_numbers.json` regenerated from a clean deletion via `uv run retraction-bench evaluate`; unchanged, since this round touched no computation, only prose and one figure's visual layout. `python -m pytest tests/`: 358 passed. `paper/latex/build.py` regenerated `main.tex`, `pdflatex`/`bibtex`/`pdflatex`x2 compiled clean. Pages 1 (Abstract), 14-15 (Methods 3.11/hypotheses/Table 1), 24 (Discussion), and 33 (Figure 9, which floats to the document's last page) rendered to PNG and read directly -- confirmed the grouped-block figure renders correctly with caption and body sharing a page, and no other visual regression.

**Consequences:** `paper/paper.md` revised in the Abstract, Introduction, section 2 (Related Work), section 3.6, section 3.11, section 3.10-referencing hypothesis block (section 4), Table 1, and section 5 (Discussion). `paper/latex/figures/make_failure_cascade.py` rewritten for the three-block grouped layout. `docs/DECISIONS.md` gained this entry and had ADR-154/155's misplaced paragraph corrected. Still open, unchanged from prior rounds: a second annotator, matched non-retracted controls, a placebo-replacement retrieval condition, clinician-paraphrased query variants, and a GPT-OSS token-budget sensitivity analysis -- none run, none required for this pass, all still named explicitly in Limitations/Future Work.

## ADR-157: Fourth external review acted on -- a Conclusion section added, H3 converted to RQ3 with a new exploratory contingency analysis, Figure 1 corrected (M1 is citation avoidance, not "use"), causal language further softened, Related Work expanded with three verified citations, forest plot rescaled

**Status:** Accepted

**Context:** A fourth external review, against `main(5).pdf` (the 33-page draft ADR-156 produced), rated it ~90-94% submission-ready -- strong on novelty, benchmark concept, and reproducibility -- and gave 5 mandatory fixes plus 5 strongly-recommended ones. This ADR is that pass, the largest single-round content addition since ADR-153's original restructuring, because two of the mandatory fixes (a missing Conclusion, and a genuinely under-supported causal comparison in H3) required new material rather than only rewording.

**Mandatory fix 1: no dedicated Conclusion section.** The manuscript went straight from Limitations to Future Work to Ethics statement, leaving the Discussion to carry the paper's final message -- a real structural gap for a benchmark paper with this many findings. Added a new section 8 ("Conclusion," ~350 words, three paragraphs: what was built, what was found, what it means) directly before the Ethics statement, which is renumbered from section 8 to section 9. The Conclusion introduces no new statistics; it restates only numbers already established in the Abstract/Results. Checked for stale cross-references to "section 7"/"section 8" elsewhere in the paper (found none needing a fix -- the one remaining "Section 7" reference correctly still points at Future Work).

**Mandatory fix 2: H3 was a hypothesis this benchmark cannot formally test.** H3 claimed superseded-evidence citation "does not reliably correspond to substantive correctness" -- phrased and treated as confirmatory, but this paper runs no single formal test of that correspondence relationship end to end. Converted to **RQ3**, a research question, matching H1/H2's own treatment as a two-hypothesis-plus-one-question confirmatory core (the hypothesis block's opening sentence rewritten accordingly). "H3 is supported by the correctness axis directly" replaced with a properly scoped statement of what the correctness analysis actually shows. **New exploratory analysis added to support RQ3 concretely, not just rhetorically**: `scripts/correctness_citation_contingency.py` cross-tabulates the 100 hand-labeled Correctness responses against M1's own citation outcome (via the same `eval.citation.cites_trap_source` function `reproduce.py` uses for every M1 number), restricted to the 50 responses under `retrieval_naive`/`retrieval_with_notice` -- the only two conditions where citation is a real, non-structural measurement, explicitly to avoid conflating "never could cite" (`no_retrieval`/`post_notice`) with "chose not to cite." Result, written to `results/correctness_citation_contingency.{json,md}` and reported in section 4.3: responses citing the trap source are correct less often (30.8%, 12/39) than responses that do not (54.5%, 6/11) -- Fisher's exact p=0.172, odds ratio 0.37, Cramér's V=0.205 -- reported explicitly as descriptive/exploratory, n=50, not reaching significance, not a confirmatory test. This is new analysis code and a new result, but zero new data collection or model calls: purely a re-analysis of already-collected, already-labeled responses.

**Mandatory fix 3: Figure 1 (and its cross-references) called M1 "USED," overstating what the metric measures.** The reviewer's precise objection: not citing the trap source does not mean the notice was used -- a model could ignore both, answer from parametric knowledge, cite a distractor, or produce a generic answer. Relabeled the middle stage of Figure 1's central chain from "Correction is USED" to "Trap-source CITATION AVOIDED," with its own subtitle restated as "M1: does the answer stop citing the superseded source?" and a new explicit caveat line added to the figure itself: "These are distinct measurable outcomes, not assumed causal stages." (`make_conceptual_figure.py`.) Every other in-text reference to the "retrieved != used != correct" framing (the item-walkthrough figure caption, Figure 1's own in-text caption) updated to "retrieved != citation avoided != correct" for consistency. The figure's own section-4.2 summary line rewritten from "retrieved -> used holds for only 1 of 3 models tested" (which implied the relationship failed to hold for the other two, when one is genuinely inconclusive, not falsified) to the precise three-way split.

**Mandatory fix 4: remaining "generation-stage failure" language still made a causal attribution the design cannot support.** The observed pattern (retrieval succeeds, citation behavior does not uniformly change) is consistent with an integration bottleneck somewhere at or after retrieval -- it does not by itself localize that bottleneck to generation specifically, when context prioritization, instruction following, document ordering, or decoding could equally be involved. Replaced "generation-stage evidence-integration failure" / "generation-stage failure" with "evidence-integration bottleneck at or downstream of retrieval" (or "after retrieval") in the abstract, the Discussion's opening paragraph, its middle paragraph, and its closing paragraph -- four occurrences, all changed identically for consistency, per the reviewer's own suggested replacement wording.

**Mandatory fix 5: abstract's "We confirm the groundedness blind spot directly" overstated a single-implementation demonstration as a confirmation.** Changed "confirm" to "demonstrate," matching the hedging already applied to every other strong claim in this abstract.

**Strongly recommended, done:**
- **Related Work expanded with three new, individually verified citations** spanning two of the reviewer's four named areas: Wallat et al. (2025, ICTIR) on the correctness-vs-faithfulness distinction in RAG attribution (directly supports this paper's own retrieved/citation-avoided/correct three-way distinction, cited in the RAG-evaluation-and-faithfulness paragraph); Ortega and Delgado-Quirós (2024, *Scientometrics*) on retraction-coverage gaps across seven scholarly databases, and the RISRS report (Schneider et al., 2022, *Research Integrity and Peer Review*) on recommended retraction-metadata infrastructure (both cited in a new "Retraction-aware infrastructure and database coverage" paragraph, directly paralleling this paper's own section 3.8 finding at the database level). **A fourth candidate citation was found and deliberately rejected**: a same-topic, same-journal (*Accountability in Research*) paper by the same author pair (Sebo & Sebo, 2025) comparing Retraction Watch/PubMed/Web of Science coverage looked like an ideal, precisely on-topic addition -- until a check for integrity concerns (a standing habit for any citation added to this project, not just this one) found that a *different* paper by the same authors on the *identical* comparison, in the *same* journal, had been formally retracted for irreproducible results the authors could not defend on request. Given no way to independently verify the surviving paper's own soundness, and the same authors' documented, topically-identical retraction, it was excluded rather than cited -- a deliberate act of the same rigor this paper asks of the systems it evaluates, and worth recording here as a demonstration that this project's own citation additions get the same verification its benchmark items do.
- **Explicit benchmark-scope sentence added** at the very start of Methods (before section 3.1): RetractionBench is stated plainly as "a hand-verified, mechanistic evaluation set, not a prevalence sample," with an explicit warning against reading any composition rate in this paper as representative of the broader population of medical retractions. The same edit also relocates the reproducibility selling-point sentence the reviewer flagged in Appendix A ("all headline evaluation results can be regenerated from cached responses and frozen hand labels, with no live API access required") to this same opening paragraph, addressing two separate review items in one place.
- **An explicit three-contribution structure added to the Introduction**, immediately after the five-finding record-quality list: a hand-verified benchmark; an evaluation framework separating three measurable stages; and empirical evidence the stages do not move together -- framed explicitly as the hierarchy under which every other finding in the paper (PubMed gaps, notice-quality coding, synthesis contamination, the NLI demonstration) sits as supporting evidence, not a flat list of unrelated results.
- **Figure 5 (the paired-effect forest plot) rescaled.** Its x-axis previously ran to +1.4 (data padded far past the actual -0.5-to+0.5 effect range) to leave room for right-side text annotations drawn in data coordinates. Reviewer feedback that this made the plotted effects look smaller than they are was correct; fixed by moving the annotations to axes-fraction coordinates (`ax.annotate(..., xycoords=("axes fraction", "data"))`), decoupling label placement from the data range entirely, and tightening the x-axis to [-0.55, 0.55] -- the qwen effect (the paper's one statistically supported result) now visibly spans nearly the plot's full width, rather than being compressed into a third of it.
- **Two named Future Work directions added** (a matched placebo-retrieval condition; replacement-evidence retrieval as a second benchmark axis, `correction retrieval -> replacement retrieval -> answer correctness`) -- both already existed as Limitations disclosures, now also stated as concrete forward-looking directions per the reviewer's request, not new commitments.
- **Diary-toned phrases trimmed further**: "not a corner cut" -> a plain statement of what the 30-minute annotation cap was predefined to standardize; "worth naming together rather than folding into one undifferentiated data-quality footnote" -> "reported individually rather than as one undifferentiated data-quality footnote"; "Models default to confidently restating..." (stated the measurement's own conclusion more strongly than its mechanical-keyword-scan method supports) -> "Explicit uncertainty was rarely detected by this predefined keyword scan," with the scan's own limitation restated in the same sentence.
- **Abstract's GPT-OSS scoping tightened** so a reader cannot read "inconclusive at this sample size" (the M1 paired test, ~71-72 responses/21-24 items) as describing the same sample as the correction-acknowledgment axis's separately-stated n=6 -- the two are now explicitly distinguished in adjacent sentences rather than left to a shared "this sample size" phrase.

**Strongly recommended, not done, and why:** a second independent annotator remains open, unchanged from every prior round -- requires human labor this pass cannot manufacture. A subtler visual separator between the "100% notice-rank" and "92% citation" bars within Figure 9's "Retrieval and use" block (the reviewer's own item 17, explicitly marked "not mandatory, just polish") was not added, to avoid undoing ADR-156's three-block grouping fix with a fourth implied sub-boundary; the caption already states the bars are independent measurements.

**Net effect on length:** this round's fixes required real new content (a Conclusion section, a working exploratory analysis with its own script and output files, three new citations with their own paragraph, a benchmark-scope statement, a three-contribution framing paragraph, two Future Work directions) rather than only tightening existing prose, unlike ADR-155's and ADR-156's rounds. `paper/paper.md` 1480 -> 1603 lines. Compiled PDF: **33 -> 35 pages**, 0 errors, 0 undefined references. This is disclosed plainly, not rounded down: the reviewer that prompted this round did not raise page count as a concern at all (unlike ADR-153's reviewers), and every addition responds to a specific mandatory or strongly-recommended fix, not padding -- but it is a real, honest increase over ADR-155's 33 pages, and should be reported as such.

**Verified, not assumed:** `python scripts/make_paper_figures.py` regenerated all figures including the corrected Figure 1 and rescaled Figure 5. `uv run python scripts/correctness_citation_contingency.py` run against the live cached data, output hand-checked against what is quoted in section 4.3 -- exact match. `results/reproduced_paper_numbers.json` regenerated from a clean deletion via `uv run retraction-bench evaluate` -- unchanged, since none of this round's M1/M3/Correctness core numbers were touched. `python -m pytest tests/`: 358 passed. `paper/latex/build.py` regenerated `main.tex`, `pdflatex`/`bibtex`/`pdflatex`x2 compiled clean. Pages 3 (Figure 1 and the three-contributions paragraph), 4 (Related Work's new citations), 6 (the Methods scope paragraph), 20 (the RQ3 contingency table), 28-29 (Future Work's new directions and the new Conclusion/renumbered Ethics statement sharing a page), and 33 (the rescaled forest plot) rendered to PNG and read directly -- confirmed correct rendering throughout, no caption/table separation, no overflow. A stale-PDF false negative was caught mid-verification: an early compile check reported "33 pages, 0 errors" from a `main.pdf` that had not actually been regenerated (a shell redirect to an already-existing, non-writable log file silently failed, `pdflatex` never re-ran, and the grep checks read a stale log) -- caught by comparing `main.tex`'s and `main.pdf`'s modification timestamps before trusting the result, not by the check itself; the real, fresh compile reported 35 pages. Documented here as a reminder to verify freshness (timestamps, or an explicit `rm` before recompiling) whenever a log-redirect command reports suspiciously convenient success.

**Consequences:** `paper/paper.md` revised in Methods (new opening scope paragraph), Introduction (new three-contributions paragraph), Related Work (new infrastructure paragraph, three new citations, two wording softenings), the hypothesis block (H3 -> RQ3), section 4.3 (new RQ3 contingency table and paragraph), Discussion (four generation-stage-language softenings), Limitations/Future Work language (two diary-toned trims, two new named directions), a new Conclusion section (8), Ethics statement renumbered (9), and the References list (three new entries). New files: `scripts/correctness_citation_contingency.py`, `results/correctness_citation_contingency.json`, `results/correctness_citation_contingency.md`. `paper/latex/figures/make_conceptual_figure.py` and `make_forest_plot.py` both edited and regenerated. Still open, unchanged from every prior round: a second independent annotator, matched non-retracted controls, a placebo-replacement retrieval condition (now also named in Future Work), replacement-evidence retrieval (now also named in Future Work), clinician-paraphrased query variants, and a GPT-OSS token-budget sensitivity analysis.

## ADR-158: Fifth external review acted on -- three citations added to paper.md's References were never wired into the actual LaTeX bibliography, found and fixed; remaining wording polish (RQ2/RQ3 hedging, Figure 9's middle-block label, diary-toned phrases)

**Status:** Accepted

**Context:** A fifth external review, against `main(6).pdf` (the 35-page draft ADR-157 produced), rated it ~94-96% submission-ready -- the highest of any round so far -- and found one genuine technical defect (not a wording preference) plus a short list of final polish items.

**The genuine bibliographic bug.** ADR-157 added three new citations (Wallat et al. 2025, Ortega and Delgado-Quirós 2024, Schneider et al./RISRS 2022) to `paper.md`'s own "## References" section and cited them in Related Work's prose. What ADR-157 missed: `paper.md`'s markdown References section is **not** the source the compiled PDF's bibliography is built from -- `paper/latex/build.py` explicitly drops that section during conversion (`# split out References (dropped -- \bibliography{refs} regenerates it)`) and instead relies on two other, separate pieces: a hand-maintained `CITE_MAP` dict in `build.py` that matches exact citation prose strings (e.g. `"(Yu et al., 2024)"`) to `\citep{}`/`\citet{}` LaTeX commands, and `paper/latex/refs.bib`, the actual BibTeX database `\bibliography{refs}` compiles against. ADR-157 added the three new citations' prose to `paper.md` but never added matching entries to `refs.bib` or `CITE_MAP` -- so the three citations rendered as inert plain text ("Wallat et al. (2025)...") in the compiled PDF, with no bibliography entry to point to, and no reviewer or reader could locate them. This is exactly the failure mode the reviewer described: "the Related Work makes specific claims based on these sources, but the reader cannot locate them." Fixed by adding all three as proper `@article`/`@inproceedings` entries to `refs.bib` (alphabetically: `ortega2024indexation`, `schneider2022reducing`, `wallat2025correctness`) and three matching `CITE_MAP` entries in `build.py` mapping the exact paper.md prose strings to `\citet{}`/`\citep{}` commands, matching the existing pattern for every other citation in the paper.

**Verified with an automated consistency check, per the reviewer's own explicit instruction ("don't manually trust your eyes"):** extracted every `\cite[pt]?{...}` key actually used in the compiled `main.tex` and every entry key actually defined in `refs.bib`, and diffed the two sorted lists. Result: exactly 17 keys on each side, zero keys cited-but-undefined, zero keys defined-but-uncited -- a perfect match, not merely "no compiler warning." This check is cheap enough (a two-line shell pipeline) that it is worth re-running after any future citation addition, though it is not (yet) wired into `make reproduce` or the test suite as an automated regression check -- a candidate for a future ADR if citations are added again.

**Remaining wording fixes, all from the reviewer's numbered "must fix" list:**
- Abstract: "We demonstrate the groundedness blind spot" -> "We empirically illustrate this groundedness blind spot," since one specific NLI implementation was tested, not a general property confirmed or demonstrated in the abstract's stronger sense.
- Discussion: "This generation-layer failure is compounded..." -> "This downstream evidence-integration problem is compounded...", removing the last remaining "generation-layer" attribution the design cannot support (ADR-157 already fixed four other "generation-stage" occurrences; this fifth one, deeper in the same paragraph, was missed).
- H2's interpretation sentence: "H2 is supported as stated: direct confrontation reliably elicits..." -> "H2 is supported descriptively: direct confrontation elicits...", and the matching Conclusion sentence "reliably elicited" -> "elicited" -- "reliably" implied an inferential claim with no formal test behind it for M3.
- `make_failure_cascade.py`'s middle block relabeled "Retrieval and use" -> "Retrieval and citation behavior" (and the matching in-text figure caption in `paper.md`), since the 92%/79.4% bars measure `cites_superseded` specifically, not "use" in the broader sense Figure 1's own earlier fix (ADR-157) already established M1 does not measure.
- Appendix A: "this project's non-negotiable minimum" -> "the prespecified minimum for this evaluation" -- the last of the diary-toned internal-project-management phrases the fourth and fifth reviews both flagged.
- Discussion: "a groundedness-style check gated on this criterion would score them as faithful" -> "a groundedness-style check using non-contradiction as its acceptance criterion would classify these outputs as passing" -- avoids overloading "faithful," a term this paper's own NLI section explains is more specific than plain non-contradiction.
- Abstract: "evaluate three open-weight and hosted language models" -> "evaluate three model/deployment configurations (open-weight and hosted)", and "the three models sit in three different evidentiary states" -> "the three model/deployment configurations exhibit three distinct empirical patterns" -- both applying this paper's own established model/deployment-configuration terminology to the abstract specifically, per the reviewer's request to standardize it there too (the more technical, already-precise "evidentiary states" phrasing elsewhere in Results/Discussion/Conclusion was left as is, since it is used consistently as a defined technical term there, not flagged by the reviewer outside the abstract).

**Not done, and why:** removing Yoshitaka Fujii's name from the Ethics statement (reviewer's item 27) -- the reviewer explicitly said "not necessary, but cleaner," not a fix; naming him is a deliberate transparency choice already justified in the text (independence-safeguard disclosure), not an oversight, so left unchanged. Reframing "retrieval succeeded" as a two-sentence Results summary (reviewer's item 23) -- a stylistic suggestion, not in the reviewer's own final numbered checklist. A broader diary-tone sweep beyond the specific phrases named (reviewer's item 9, generic) -- judged already adequately addressed across ADR-154/155/156/157's repeated passes on this exact issue; diminishing returns from further unprompted sweeping.

**Verified, not assumed:** the citation/bibliography consistency check described above (17/17 exact match). `python scripts/make_paper_figures.py` regenerated the failure-cascade figure with its corrected label. `uv run retraction-bench evaluate` re-run from a clean deletion -- unchanged, no computation touched this round. `python -m pytest tests/`: 358 passed. `paper/latex/build.py` regenerated `main.tex`; `pdflatex`/`bibtex`/`pdflatex`x2 compiled clean -- 0 errors, 0 undefined references, confirmed against a freshly-timestamped `main.pdf` (not a stale one, per ADR-157's own documented lesson about verifying compile freshness). Page count unchanged at 35. Pages 1 (abstract), 15 (H1/H2/RQ3 block), 25-26 (Discussion's two remaining fixes), 30 (the bibliography page itself, all three new entries confirmed present and correctly alphabetized and accented), and 31 (Appendix A's "prespecified minimum") rendered to PNG and read directly.

**Consequences:** `paper/latex/refs.bib` gained three entries; `paper/latex/build.py`'s `CITE_MAP` gained three entries. `paper/paper.md` revised in the Abstract (three wording fixes), the hypothesis-interpretation paragraph, the Conclusion, the Discussion (two fixes), Appendix A, and the Figure 9 caption. `paper/latex/figures/make_failure_cascade.py` relabeled and regenerated. This is the first round in this paper's revision history where an external review's core finding was a build/tooling defect (a citation silently failing to reach the bibliography) rather than a scientific-wording issue -- worth remembering that adding a citation to this project requires touching three places (`paper.md`'s prose, `paper.md`'s markdown References list for human readers, and `refs.bib`+`CITE_MAP` for the actual compiled bibliography), not just the first two.

## ADR-159: Sixth external review acted on -- final six-item wording checklist (causal-absolutism fix in the Introduction's opening paragraph, rhetorical-language softening, global "evidentiary states" -> "empirical patterns" replacement, abstract benchmark-composition disclosure)

**Status:** Accepted

**Context:** A sixth external review, against `main(7).pdf` (the 35-page draft ADR-158 produced), rated it ~96-98% submission-ready and gave a six-item "final pre-submission edits" checklist with an explicit instruction: "do these six edits and stop." No further conceptual or structural revision was requested; this ADR is exactly that six-item pass, plus three additional one-line fixes the same review called out with exact suggested wording (a Figure 1 caption sentence, a Figure 8 caption sentence, and the abstract's `"maximally dangerous"` -- three near-zero-risk, high-precision fixes bundled into this same pass rather than deferred to a seventh round for three trivial sentences).

**Checklist item 1: the Introduction's opening paragraph overstated where the failure occurs.** "The failure is not in the generation step at all" contradicted the paper's own later finding that citation behavior diverges specifically during generation/evidence-integration (Discussion, section 5) -- the reviewer's own summary: "you're essentially saying later: retrieval succeeded, but the model didn't consistently integrate the correction." Replaced with the reviewer's own preferred wording: "The problem is not simply whether the model can summarize the retrieved document, but whether the system recognizes that the retrieved source no longer represents the current state of the evidence." The adjacent "Groundedness has no mechanism for detecting this, by construction" was tightened in the same edit to "Conventional groundedness evaluation has no explicit mechanism for detecting this, because it evaluates correspondence between the answer and the retrieved document rather than the document's current validity" -- closing the same "RAG could in principle be augmented" objection ADR-157 already closed in the abstract, now applied to the Introduction's own parallel sentence.

**Checklist item 2: the abstract's benchmark-composition sentence omitted the one Rehabilitation/Therapy item.** The abstract said "28 retracted medical claims across pharmacology and obstetrics/gynecology" without mentioning the single Rehabilitation/Therapy item this benchmark deliberately retains (section 3.3 explains why: a fully-annotated item is kept rather than discarded when its domain is later retired). Changed to "28 retracted medical claims, primarily across pharmacology and obstetrics/gynecology (with one rehabilitation/therapy item retained individually)," the reviewer's own preferred of the two options offered.

**Checklist items 3 and (partially) 7: "maximally dangerous" was rhetorically stronger than a Q1 paper's register warrants**, appearing twice (abstract, Discussion). Both changed to "clinically unsafe" ("a groundedness check while producing a clinically unsafe answer"; "a perfectly grounded citation of a retracted document is a clinically unsafe output"), the reviewer's own preferred phrasing over the alternative "unsafe evidence claim."

**Checklist item 4: "three different evidentiary states" replaced globally with "three distinct empirical patterns," per the reviewer's explicit instruction to make this replacement everywhere** (not just the abstract, which ADR-158 had already fixed). Four remaining occurrences fixed: the Results section's introduction to Table 3 ("The models show three distinct empirical patterns, not two"), Figure 5's own caption, the Conclusion's summary sentence, and a Limitations bullet describing the same paired test. Rationale unchanged from the reviewer's own explanation: "evidentiary state" implies a formal classification this paper never defines, while "empirical pattern" states exactly what was measured.

**Checklist item 5: "a second, independent failure one layer upstream" overstated statistical/causal independence the design cannot establish.** The two failures (evidence-integration bottleneck; synthesis contamination) are analytically distinct, not necessarily statistically or causally independent of each other. Changed "independent" to "distinct" in the Discussion's compounding-failures paragraph, and applied the identical fix to the following sentence's "a third, independent axis" -> "a third, distinct axis" for internal consistency (the reviewer named only the first instance; the second uses identical, equally overstated language one sentence later).

**Checklist item 6: "remove a little of the defensive/process-history language from the Introduction"** -- addressed as part of item 1's paragraph rewrite above, which removed the "by construction" framing alongside the causal-absolutism fix; judged sufficient for this round given the reviewer's own explicit six-item cap and repeated prior rounds' (ADR-154 through ADR-158) already-substantial work on this exact issue elsewhere in the manuscript.

**Three additional one-line fixes bundled in, each with the reviewer's own exact suggested replacement text, each a single sentence, each carrying negligible risk:**
- Figure 1's embedded caption text (inside `make_conceptual_figure.py`'s matplotlib annotation, not the LaTeX caption): "correctness is lower than citation-avoidance behavior alone would suggest, across every condition" -> "citation avoidance and substantive correctness diverge across the evaluated conditions" -- removes the counterfactual "would suggest" framing.
- Figure 8's caption: "only 3 distinct integer coordinate pairs occur across 24 items, so jitter is essential here, not decorative" -> "because only three distinct integer coordinate pairs occur, jitter is used to reveal overlapping observations" -- removes the mildly defensive "not decorative."

**Not done, and explicitly out of scope for this round:** the Introduction's further structural compression (reviewer's own item 9, restructuring the five-finding list into a single summary sentence with numbers deferred to Results) -- the reviewer's own words: "this isn't a serious problem anymore," explicitly not part of the six-item "do these and stop" checklist, and re-litigating the five-finding list's placement in the Introduction has been an explicit, deliberate editorial decision since ADR-153 (the findings are cited back from the Abstract, Discussion, and Limitations). Removing Yoshitaka Fujii's name from the Ethics statement (raised again implicitly via item 10's general framing, first raised explicitly in the fifth review) -- still not a fix per either reviewer's own words, still a deliberate transparency disclosure, not an oversight.

**Verified, not assumed:** `python scripts/make_paper_figures.py` regenerated all figures including the corrected Figure 1 caption text. `uv run retraction-bench evaluate` re-run from a clean deletion -- unchanged, no computation touched this round. `python -m pytest tests/`: 358 passed. `paper/latex/build.py` regenerated `main.tex`; `pdflatex`/`bibtex`/`pdflatex`x2 compiled clean -- 0 errors, 0 undefined references, confirmed against a freshly-timestamped `main.pdf`. The citation/bibliography consistency check (every `\cite[pt]?{}` key in `main.tex` diffed against every key in `refs.bib`) re-run and still a perfect 17/17 match, confirming this round's edits did not disturb ADR-158's bibliography fix. Page count unchanged at 35. Pages 1 (abstract: "clinically unsafe," the rehabilitation/therapy disclosure), 2 (Introduction's rewritten opening paragraph), 3 (Figure 1's corrected embedded caption text, rendered inside the image itself, not just the LaTeX caption), and 26 (Discussion's "distinct failure"/"distinct axis" fixes) rendered to PNG and read directly -- confirmed correct rendering throughout, no regressions.

**Consequences:** `paper/paper.md` revised in the Abstract (benchmark composition, "clinically unsafe"), Introduction (opening paragraph rewrite), Results (Table 3 lead-in, a Limitations bullet), Discussion ("clinically unsafe," "distinct failure," "distinct axis"), Conclusion (empirical-patterns wording), and two figure captions (Figure 5, Figure 8). `paper/latex/figures/make_conceptual_figure.py` edited (Figure 1's embedded text) and regenerated. This closes the sixth reviewer's full checklist; per that review's own final verdict ("SUBMISSION READY... not inventing new methodology"), no further scientific or structural revision is anticipated from this line of external review -- any further rounds should be expected to surface proofreading-level items only, not new mandatory fixes, barring a genuinely new critique.

## ADR-160: Seventh external review acted on -- three mandatory terminology fixes ("independent" -> "distinct" layers, "three stages" -> "three measurable outcomes," a Discussion sentence scoped precisely to what M1 measures

**Status:** Accepted

**Context:** A seventh external review, against `main(8).pdf` (the 35-page draft ADR-159 produced), rated it ~98% submission-ready and gave exactly three mandatory edits, all one-line terminology precision fixes, with an explicit closing recommendation to do a final numerical/reference/cross-reference audit and venue-format conversion after these three and then "stop changing the science." This ADR is that three-item pass.

**Fix 1: "Two independent layers of failure emerge" (the Abstract's opening structural sentence) risked a pedantic misreading.** The word "independent" in context meant "independent of any AI model" (the record-level layer exists regardless of what any model does), not "statistically independent of the second layer" -- a reviewer could read it as a stronger claim than intended. Changed to "Two distinct layers of failure emerge," matching the word already used correctly nearby ("independent of any model," left unchanged, since that usage is unambiguous in its own sentence).

**Fix 2: "three stages" implied a causal pipeline the paper explicitly disclaims one sentence later ("not assumed causal stages").** This was genuinely self-inflicted terminology trouble: the Introduction's three-contributions paragraph and the Conclusion's opening paragraph both introduced the framework as "three stages," immediately followed by "not assumed causal stages" -- calling something a stage and then saying it is not a stage in the next breath. Both occurrences changed to "three measurable outcomes" (the Introduction's "these stages do not move together" changed to "these outcomes do not move together" in the same edit for internal consistency). The negated phrase "not assumed causal stages" itself was left unchanged in both the Introduction and Figure 1's own caption/embedded text -- that phrase is a deliberate disclaimer contrasting against the causal-pipeline reading, not an instance of the same terminology problem, and remains correct as written.

**Fix 3: a Discussion sentence in section 4.5 attributed a broader capability failure than M1 actually measures.** "At least one model (and plausibly two) fails to reliably use a notice that is ranked 1st, 2nd, or 3rd..." could be read as a general claim about notice comprehension or use, when M1 measures one specific, narrow thing: whether the answer stops citing the trap source. Changed to the reviewer's own suggested wording: "at least one evaluated model did not consistently translate the retrieved notice into citation avoidance, despite that notice ranking 1st, 2nd, or 3rd..." The paragraph's closing sentence -- "The notice is not hard to find. For some models, it is hard to get used once found." -- was deliberately left untouched despite also using "used": an earlier external review (documented in this project's own memory, prior to ADR-153) explicitly flagged this as the single most memorable sentence in the paper and asked that it be preserved; this review did not flag it, and rewriting a memorable, already-explicitly-protected sentence on this round's own initiative would contradict that earlier explicit instruction without a matching request to revisit it.

**Noted but not actioned, per the reviewer's own explicit guidance:** the abstract's information density (reviewer: "not a scientific flaw... I would not rewrite the whole abstract now," offered only as a conditional cut if a venue's abstract limit requires it) and the 35-page length (reviewer: "purely a venue-format issue... not a scientific one," contingent entirely on the eventual target venue's own page limits, which are not yet chosen). Neither is a text change to make now; both are flagged for the user's own venue-selection decision, consistent with this project's standing principle of not forcing a length or format change the actual submission target hasn't yet specified.

**Verified, not assumed:** `python scripts/make_paper_figures.py` was not re-run this round (no figure or figure-embedded text was touched -- confirmed by grepping every figure script for "independent layers," "three stages," and "fails to reliably use," none matched). `uv run retraction-bench evaluate` re-run from a clean deletion -- unchanged, no computation touched. `python -m pytest tests/`: 358 passed. `paper/latex/build.py` regenerated `main.tex`; `pdflatex`/`bibtex`/`pdflatex`x2 compiled clean -- 0 errors, 0 undefined references, confirmed against a freshly-timestamped `main.pdf`. The citation/bibliography consistency check re-run and still a perfect 17/17 match. Page count unchanged at 35. Pages 1 (abstract's "distinct layers"), 3 (the three-contributions paragraph's "measurable outcomes," Figure 1's own page), and 22 (section 4.5's rescoped Discussion sentence, with the memorable closing sentence confirmed unchanged immediately after it) rendered to PNG and read directly.

**Consequences:** `paper/paper.md` revised in the Abstract (one word), the Introduction's three-contributions paragraph (two phrases), the Conclusion's opening paragraph (one phrase), and section 4.5 (one sentence rescoped). No figures, tables, code, or reported statistics changed -- the smallest single-round diff of any external-review pass in this paper's revision history (three sentences, four total word/phrase substitutions). Per the reviewer's own final assessment ("the manuscript has crossed the point where additional tinkering is more likely to create inconsistencies than improve it"), the next appropriate action on a future round of feedback is a numerical/reference/cross-reference audit and venue-format conversion, not further prose revision, unless a genuinely new critique surfaces.

## ADR-161: Eighth external review acted on -- an eventual literature-competition claim independently verified before acting on it (5 of 6 cited papers real and added to Related Work; 1 -- the single most damaging one -- could not be verified and appears fabricated); large-scope suggestions (new formal metrics, matched controls, placebo condition, more annotators, frontier models) explicitly declined without new resources

**Status:** Accepted

**Context:** An eighth external review, against `main(9).pdf` (the 36-page draft ADR-160 produced), reversed its own prior ~98% assessment, arguing that the manuscript's novelty is insufficient for a top ML venue on two grounds: (1) no mathematical formulation, novel metric, or formal theory, and (2) the literature has "moved underneath" the manuscript, citing six specific 2025-2026 papers as evidence the "comparatively unstudied gap" claim is now false, one of which it described as "actually larger and broader" than RetractionBench (100 items, 14 frontier LLMs, 8,400 judgments). It also proposed a set of formal replacements: a "Supersession-Aware Groundedness" framework with invented metrics (SAG, DGR, CEG), a matched-placebo causal design with an ATE estimand, a hierarchical logistic model, matched non-retracted controls, 2-3 independent annotators, and frontier-model coverage.

**This review's citations were independently verified before any paper change was made** -- a deliberate step, not a formality, given this project's standing non-negotiable rule against fabrication and the immediately preceding round's own finding (a prior review's Sebo & Sebo citation, integrity-checked and rejected rather than cited). Five of the six citations were confirmed real via direct web search against publisher/preprint sources, with exact author lists, venues, volumes, and page numbers verified: Labenbacher et al. (2026, *JMIR* 28:e88766), Thelwall (2026, arXiv:2604.16872), Metze et al. (2025, *Journal of Clinical Anesthesia* 107:112032), Yao et al. (2025, *The Science of Nature* 112), and Rodionov and Assylbekov (2026, arXiv:2608.11415, TRACES). **The sixth -- "Xue Zhang et al.'s Trojan Paper Medical Benchmark," SSRN abstract_id=7018684, described as 100 retracted medical RCTs and 14 frontier LLMs -- could not be found anywhere**: the named SSRN abstract ID does not resolve to any paper (a direct search confirms "The abstract ID 7018684 does not appear to exist in the SSRN database"), and no trace of the named authors, title, or benchmark exists in any indexed source, despite this being the review's single most consequential claim (the one paper alleged to be "larger and broader on the model side" than RetractionBench, used to argue the benchmark itself is scooped). This citation is treated as unverified and not fabricated-and-cited: it was not added to Related Work, and no claim in this paper is built on it. This is disclosed here plainly rather than quietly dropped, because the review's overall argument leaned on it disproportionately, and a future reader of this review chain deserves to know that one of its six supporting citations did not survive a basic existence check.

**What was genuinely actionable, and done.** The five verified papers are real, contemporaneous, and directly relevant: they establish that direct LLM knowledge of retraction status (asked outside a RAG pipeline, e.g. "is this paper retracted?") is now a well-studied, convergently-answered question, which the Introduction's and Related Work's "comparatively unstudied gap" language did not fully anticipate. Added a new Related Work paragraph, "Concurrent studies directly probing LLM knowledge of retraction status," summarizing all five accurately and specifically (sample sizes, methods, findings, each individually cited), immediately followed by an explicit, honest distinguishing claim: none of the five is structured as a controlled RAG-pipeline evaluation -- each asks whether a model recognizes retraction status when queried about a specific paper directly, not whether corrective evidence already surfaced by retrieval changes an otherwise-unprompted generation pipeline's citation and correctness behavior. This is RetractionBench's actual, still-defensible distinguishing design choice (holding retrieval conditions fixed and controlled so retrieval availability, citation avoidance, and substantive correctness can be measured as three separate outcomes with paired statistical testing), not the broader and now-inaccurate claim that no one has studied AI-and-retraction at all. The "Positioning" paragraph and the table's lead-in sentence were both rewritten to match this narrower, still-true claim rather than the broader one the concurrent literature now answers. All five citations added properly through all three required places (`paper.md` prose, `paper.md`'s markdown References list, `refs.bib` + `build.py`'s `CITE_MAP`) -- the exact process ADR-158 established after a prior round skipped the last of these three.

**What was correctly identified but explicitly declined, and why:**
- **Inventing new formal metrics (SAG, DGR, CEG) or a hierarchical logistic model.** The reviewer's own formulas are unvalidated inventions proposed for rhetorical effect ("the exact formula needs to be developed properly, not invented because reviewers enjoy Greek letters" -- the review's own words, immediately followed by inventing formulas anyway). Adopting them wholesale, without validating them against real data or establishing they measure something the paper's existing categorical framework does not already report, would itself be exactly the kind of fabricated-methodological-sophistication this project's standing rules prohibit. The paper's existing framework (M1, M3, Correctness, cross-tabulated in the RQ3 contingency analysis) already reports every distinction the reviewer's R/U/V/C states describe; formalizing it as boxed equations would add notation, not new measurement.
- **A matched placebo-retrieval condition, matched non-retracted controls, a second/third annotator, and frontier-model coverage.** All four require genuinely new experiments, human labor, or paid model access this pass cannot manufacture -- unchanged from every prior round's identical conclusion (ADR-151 onward), and already named explicitly in Limitations and Future Work. No new experiment was fabricated to answer this review's request for them.
- **A full reframing of the paper's central identity** (the reviewer's suggested "supersession-aware evaluation framework" repositioning). This is a legitimate strategic question -- about target venue, scope of future investment, and whether to treat this as an honestly-scoped benchmark/evaluation paper (which it is) versus attempting a larger pivot toward a methods paper (which would require the new experiments above) -- and was surfaced to the author as a decision rather than executed unilaterally.

**Verified, not assumed:** the citation-existence check for all six papers (five confirmed via multiple independent search results with matching authors/venues/findings; one confirmed absent via a direct SSRN abstract-ID lookup returning no result). `uv run retraction-bench evaluate` re-run from a clean deletion -- unchanged, no computation touched. `python -m pytest tests/`: 358 passed. `paper/latex/build.py` regenerated `main.tex`; `pdflatex`/`bibtex`/`pdflatex`x2 compiled clean -- 0 errors, 0 undefined references, confirmed against a freshly-timestamped `main.pdf`. The citation/bibliography consistency check (every `\cite[pt]?{}` key in `main.tex` diffed against every key in `refs.bib`) re-run and expanded to a perfect 22/22 match (up from 17/17), confirming all five new citations landed correctly in all three required places. Page count: 35 -> 36 (one page, from the new Related Work paragraph and five new bibliography entries). The new paragraph's own page and the two-page bibliography spread containing all five new entries were rendered to PNG and read directly, confirming correct rendering, alphabetization, and accented-character display.

**Consequences:** `paper/paper.md` revised in Related Work (one new paragraph, the "Positioning" paragraph rewritten, the post-table summary sentence rewritten, the References list). `paper/latex/refs.bib` gained five entries; `build.py`'s `CITE_MAP` gained five entries. This is the first round in this paper's revision history driven by a claim about competing literature rather than the paper's own internal wording or structure, and the first round where a cited paper failed an independent existence check -- both are disclosed here as a matter of record, and the same verify-before-acting discipline should apply to any future review that cites external literature, not only this one. The larger strategic question this review raised (whether to invest in new experiments toward a stronger methods-paper framing, or submit the current honestly-scoped benchmark/evaluation paper as is) remains open and was left to the author's own decision, not resolved by this ADR.

## ADR-162: Final numerical/reference/cross-reference audit -- two real figure-number cross-reference bugs found and fixed (Table 3 and Table 4 both pointed at the wrong figure), plus a systematic verification pass finding no further defects

**Status:** Accepted

**Context:** Following the author's explicit instruction to finish the manuscript, and per the seventh external review's own recommendation (act on its three wording fixes, then do "a final numerical/reference/cross-reference audit," not further prose revision), this ADR is that audit: a systematic pass checking every section cross-reference, every figure and table number, and internal consistency of every recurring headline statistic across the 36-page manuscript -- not a review-response round, self-initiated.

**Method.** (1) Extracted every `section N`/`section N.M` reference in `paper.md` via regex and checked each target number against the actual section header list -- 27 distinct section numbers referenced, all resolve to real headers, none stale (in particular, no leftover reference to the old numbering from before the Conclusion was inserted as section 8 in ADR-157). (2) Extracted every `Table N` reference and confirmed exactly four tables exist, numbered 1-4, each referenced correctly. (3) Extracted every `Figure N` reference and cross-checked against the actual document order of the nine figure embeds (`fig_conceptual_framework`=1, `fig_funnel`=2, `fig_conditions`=3, `fig_m1_by_model`=4, `fig_forest_paired_m1`=5, `fig_item_walkthrough`=6, `fig_m3_by_model`=7, `fig_retrievability_panels`=8, `fig_failure_cascade`=9). (4) Grepped every recurring headline statistic (7.4%/27-364, 97.2%/352-362, 24/24, 3 of 19/3-19, seed-agreement counts, M3's 84/38/28, Correctness's 40.0%, the 14/13/1 domain composition, the 900-response formula, retrieval-ceiling correlation values) across all the locations it appears and confirmed every occurrence matches.

**Two real bugs found and fixed, both figure-number cross-references that had gone stale across the many rounds of figure additions/reordering in ADR-153 through ADR-161, never caught by any prior external review or by the LaTeX compiler (a wrong figure number is not a compile error):**
- **Table 3's caption** ("Primary M1 result... also shown as Figure 4") pointed at the M1-by-model bar chart, but the figure immediately following Table 3 in the document -- and the one actually described as "Table 3's paired-difference table shown as a forest plot" in its own caption -- is `fig_forest_paired_m1`, Figure 5. Fixed to "also shown as Figure 5."
- **Table 4's caption** ("Evidence-record failure modes... see also Figure 5") pointed at the paired-effect forest plot, but the parenthetical's own wording ("not one shrinking population") is the failure-cascade figure's own defining language, `fig_failure_cascade`, Figure 9. Fixed to "see also Figure 9."
- **A third, matching bug in `paper/supplementary.md`** (S5, full retrieval-ranking results): "the live, corrected retrieval-ceiling measurement used throughout section 4.5 and Figure 6" -- Figure 6 is the RB-0001 item-walkthrough figure (single-item answer text, no ranking data at all); the figure that actually visualizes per-item trap-source rank and notice rank is `fig_retrievability_panels`, Figure 8. Fixed to "Figure 8."

No other defects found. All 27 section references, all 4 table references, and all recurring statistics checked out consistent on first pass -- the two table-caption bugs above are believed to be the only surviving cross-reference errors, caught because this was the first round to systematically extract and check every reference mechanically rather than reading prose in isolation.

**Verified, not assumed:** `paper/latex/build.py` regenerated `main.tex`; `pdflatex`/`bibtex`/`pdflatex`x2 compiled clean -- 0 errors, 0 undefined references, confirmed against a freshly-timestamped `main.pdf`. The citation/bibliography consistency check re-run and remains a perfect 22/22 match (unaffected, since this round touched no citations). `uv run retraction-bench evaluate` re-run from a clean deletion -- unchanged, no computation touched. `python -m pytest tests/`: 358 passed. Page count unchanged at 36 (both fixes were single-digit corrections, no prose added or removed). Both corrected table captions rendered to PNG and read directly on their actual pages (Table 3 on page 18, Table 4 on page 11), confirming the correct figure numbers now display and the fix did not disturb either table's own formatting.

**Consequences:** `paper/paper.md` revised in two table captions (Table 3, Table 4); `paper/supplementary.md` revised in one cross-reference (S5). No figures, statistics, or section content changed. This closes the seventh external review's own recommended final step ("a final numerical/reference/cross-reference audit and venue-format conversion") for the numerical/reference/cross-reference portion; venue-format conversion (adapting to a specific target journal's LaTeX class, column layout, and page limit) remains open and requires the author to first choose a target venue, which has not yet been done.

## ADR-163: Ninth external review acted on -- a real replacement-evidence retrieval measurement added by reusing already-built corpus/index infrastructure (no new model calls, no new annotation); matched controls, a placebo-retrieval condition, and a second annotator explicitly declined for lack of credentials/resources, not judgment

**Status:** Accepted

**Context:** A ninth external review, against `main(10).pdf` (the 36-page draft ADR-162 produced), gave a 7-item priority-ranked list explicitly aimed at strengthening the paper's novelty without inventing decorative mathematics -- closer in spirit to the eighth review's substance than its execution. Four items were ranked highest-impact: a new formal supersession metric, a matched non-retracted control, a placebo-retrieval condition, and a replacement-retrieval metric. This ADR evaluates all seven against what this session can actually do without fabricating data, spending the author's money, or claiming credentials this environment does not have -- not against what would look most impressive.

**Feasibility was checked directly, not assumed, before deciding what to do.** `env` showed no `GROQ_API_KEY` configured -- the route `openai/gpt-oss-120b` runs through -- so a new experimental condition requiring live calls to that model cannot run in this session regardless of any other consideration. A local `ollama` binary is present but `curl localhost:11434/api/tags` returned zero pulled models, meaning Llama/Qwen inference would first require downloading multi-gigabyte model weights. Against this, the corpus (6.7GB, already assembled and frozen), its FAISS index, and the `BAAI/bge-large-en-v1.5` embedding model were all confirmed present and loadable **fully offline** (`HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1` load succeeded in seconds). This asymmetry -- retrieval-only measurements are free and already possible; anything requiring new LLM generation is blocked on credentials/downloads this session does not have -- is what actually separates the seven proposed changes into "do now" and "needs the author's own resources," not a judgment call about which the review's authors would consider more important.

**Item 5 (replacement-retrieval metric): done, for real, using only existing infrastructure.** The corpus already contains each benchmark item's current/replacement evidence, inserted once during construction with `corpus_role = "superseding_evidence"` and linked via `linked_source_registry_id = evidence.identifier` (`corpus/assemble.py`, confirmed by direct inspection, not assumed from documentation) -- this was already-collected data nobody had queried this way yet, not a new experiment. Wrote `scripts/replacement_retrieval_ceiling.py`, extending exactly the pattern `corpus/retrieval_ceiling.py` already established for trap-source/notice ranks: for each of the 15 items with `item_type = superseded_with_current_answer`, find the rank of its own superseding-evidence document(s) in the full, brute-force similarity ranking over the live 325,167-document corpus. Result, added to section 4.5 as a new paragraph (not a new numbered subsection, to avoid re-triggering the cross-reference-renumbering risk ADR-162 just finished auditing): recall@1 = 6.7% (1/15), recall@3 = 53.3%, recall@10 = 60.0% and flat beyond that -- a sharp asymmetry with the 24/24-at-rank-3 near-certainty the trap source and its own notice show. Twelve of 15 items rank acceptably (rank <=56); three (RB-0010, RB-0012, RB-0013) rank at position 7,941 / 81,153 / 253,313 -- effectively unfindable, not merely subordinate. This is a real, striking, non-cherry-picked negative finding that directly strengthens the paper's own central claim (avoiding the wrong evidence and finding the right evidence are different problems) using data this project already owned. Limitations' "no direct evaluation of replacement-evidence retrieval" bullet was split into two, since it is now half-true: the retrieval side is measured; the generation side (does a model's answer actually cite the correct replacement, not just avoid the retracted source) remains open, correctly re-scoped rather than left overstated. Future Work's matching bullet updated the same way.
