**Item 2 (a new formal metric): the underlying substance already exists in the RQ3 contingency table; no new named formula was introduced.** The review's own words acknowledge the risk here ("do not invent a decorative equation... formalize your central phenomenon"), but its own proposed `SGR`/`SAG`/`DGR` notation is exactly the kind of unvalidated invention ADR-161 already declined once this session, for the same reason: naming a quantity with a Greek-letter-flavored acronym does not make it a validated metric, and this paper's standing discipline (going back to M2's own removal, section 3.10) is to drop a metric rather than compute it with an uncalibrated or decorative formula. The specific quantity the review wants -- how often a response is grounded in retracted evidence *and* wrong -- is already directly reported in `results/correctness_citation_contingency.json`'s existing 2x2 table (ADR-157): 25 of 50 retrieval-eligible hand-labeled responses cite the trap source and are incorrect. No new sentence was added restating this under a new name; it is judged already adequately reported, and inventing formal notation for a quantity the paper already states in a table would add the exact kind of surface-level mathematical dressing this project's own standing rules, and the review's own words, both caution against.

**Item 1 (one central claim) and item 7 (Figure 1 as the signature figure): judged already done, not re-executed.** The Conclusion's closing paragraph already states, nearly verbatim, the exact claim the review proposes as the paper's organizing idea ("retrieving corrective evidence is not equivalent to a system avoiding the superseded citation it corrects, and avoiding that citation is not equivalent to producing a substantively correct answer," ADR-157); the Introduction's three-contributions paragraph (ADR-157) already frames every other finding as evidence supporting that claim, not a competing contribution; Figure 1 already carries the "distinct measurable outcomes, not assumed causal stages" framing the review asks for (ADR-157, tightened further in ADR-160/161). Re-executing either would mean rewriting already-well-tested, multiply-reviewed prose against no new information, risking exactly the kind of inconsistency-introducing churn the seventh review warned against. Not touched this round.

**Items 3, 4, and 6 (matched non-retracted control, placebo-retrieval condition, second annotator): explicitly declined, and the reason is resource/credential availability, not disagreement with the review's judgment that these would strengthen the paper.** All three would be real, substantial improvements -- this is not contested. All three require something this session does not have: item 3 requires new candidate-item sourcing and verification, the same multi-hour annotation labor every prior round has declined for the same reason since ADR-151; item 4 requires new LLM generation calls this session is credential-blocked from making (no Groq key) and infrastructure-blocked from making cheaply (no Ollama models pulled, requiring a multi-gigabyte download before any call could even be attempted); item 6 requires a second human being's real clinical/domain judgment, which cannot be manufactured by this or any other automated pass. None were attempted, fabricated, or approximated with synthetic substitutes.

**Verified, not assumed:** `scripts/replacement_retrieval_ceiling.py` run twice, back to back, producing byte-identical per-item ranks both times (the embedding model and FAISS search are deterministic, no seed needed) -- confirming the result is not an artifact of a single run. `python scripts/make_paper_figures.py` was not re-run (no figure touched). `uv run retraction-bench evaluate` re-run from a clean deletion -- unchanged, since this round's new script is independent of the core M1/M3/Correctness pipeline. `python -m pytest tests/`: 358 passed. `paper/latex/build.py` regenerated `main.tex`; `pdflatex`/`bibtex`/`pdflatex`x2 compiled clean -- 0 errors, 0 undefined references, confirmed against a freshly-timestamped `main.pdf`. The citation/bibliography consistency check re-run and remains a perfect 22/22 match (unaffected, no citations touched). Page count: 36 -> 37 (one page, from the new section 4.5 paragraph and the split Limitations bullet). The new paragraph's own page and the updated Limitations/Future Work pages rendered to PNG and read directly.

**Consequences:** `paper/paper.md` revised in section 4.5 (new paragraph), Limitations (one bullet split into two), and Future Work (one bullet rewritten). New files: `scripts/replacement_retrieval_ceiling.py`, `results/replacement_retrieval_ceiling.json`, `results/replacement_retrieval_ceiling.md`. No existing figures, tables, or core-pipeline statistics changed. This is the first round in this paper's revision history to add a genuinely new empirical measurement in direct response to external review -- distinct from ADR-157's RQ3 contingency analysis (a re-analysis of already-collected model responses) in that it queries the corpus fresh, but identical in spirit: real infrastructure this project already owns, reused honestly, with the result reported exactly as it came out (including the three catastrophic-rank items, not smoothed over). The three declined items (matched controls, placebo condition, second annotator) remain named explicitly in Limitations and Future Work as open, resource-gated follow-on work, unchanged in substance from every prior round since ADR-151 -- only this ADR's stated reason for declining them (checked, not assumed, infrastructure/credential availability) is new.

## ADR-164: Tenth external review acted on -- a generation-side replacement-citation check built from already-cached responses (no new model calls), a compact indicator-variable notation added for the already-measured failure chain (not a new validated metric), the finding elevated into the Abstract; Figure 1 redesign, matched controls, placebo condition, second annotator, and a title change all explicitly deferred to the author

**Status:** Accepted

**Context:** A tenth external review, against a freshly re-exported `main(...).pdf` reflecting ADR-163's replacement-retrieval-ceiling addition, judged the paper's architecture had "not yet caught up" with that new result and pressed on four of the same three items ADR-163 already declined (matched control, placebo condition, generation-side replacement citation) plus three new asks: integrate replacement retrieval into Figure 1 and a formal notation, move the finding into the Abstract, and consider retitling the paper around "supersession-aware groundedness." This ADR is that pass.

**The generation-side replacement-citation gap (this review's item D, and the ninth review's item before it) turned out to be closeable with zero new experiments, on closer inspection.** ADR-163 declined it on the assumption that closing it required a new kind of measurement; checking the actual cached data directly showed otherwise. Every one of the 900 cached `ModelResponse` records already stores its own `retrieved_document_ids` -- the specific top-5 PMIDs that specific response's own retrieval actually returned, not merely a corpus-wide ceiling -- and `rag/parse.py`'s `parse_response` already extracts `cited_document_ids` from `raw_answer_text`, the same parsing `eval/citation.py` uses for M1. Nothing about this required a new model call; it required asking a new question of an answer already sitting in the cache. Wrote `scripts/replacement_citation_check.py`, joining these two already-recorded fields per response for every `retrieval_naive`/`retrieval_with_notice` response belonging to the 13 items that are both current-answer-eligible and retrieval-eligible (RB-0026/RB-0027 have current replacement evidence but no recoverable notice text, so they never enter these two conditions at all -- discovered and disclosed as part of this check, not assumed). Result: of 234 scored responses, the replacement evidence was actually present in the response's own retrieved context in 126 (53.8%, an all-or-nothing pattern per item since the retrieval query is fixed and only generation varies by seed -- 7 items always have it, 6 never do); of those 126, only 79 (62.7%) were cited. RB-0018 is the sharpest single case: replacement evidence retrieved in all 18 of its responses, cited in zero. Added to section 4.5 as a second new paragraph (after ADR-163's retrieval-ceiling paragraph); the Limitations bullet ADR-163 wrote was updated again to reflect that citation, not just retrieval, is now measured, with the one honestly-remaining gap (whether citing the replacement corresponds to higher Correctness) checked and confirmed too small to estimate reliably -- the Correctness hand-label sample and this check's own sample overlap at exactly n=12, verified by direct set intersection, not asserted.

**A compact indicator-variable notation was added for the already-measured failure chain -- deliberately not a new validated metric.** The review's own request ("give your framework formal variables... I would not manufacture mathematics") was taken at its word: five binary outcomes already reported throughout the paper on their own denominators ($R_c$ notice retrieved, $R_r$ replacement retrieved, $U_s$ trap source used/cited = M1, $U_r$ replacement cited, $C$ substantively correct) were named, not combined into any new aggregate score. The notation exists only to name one concretely observed state precisely: $R_c=1, R_r=1, U_s=0, U_r=0$ -- correction available, replacement available, trap source correctly avoided, replacement still not cited -- and to point at RB-0018 as a real, non-hypothetical instance of it. Added to the Discussion, immediately before the closing "taken together" paragraph. **This required one real code change**: `paper/latex/build.py`'s markdown-to-LaTeX converter had no math-mode support (its `escape_plain` function escapes `$` to `\$`, which would have rendered `$R_c$` as a literal escaped dollar sign, not a subscripted italic variable) -- added a `$...$` inline-math token handler, following the exact pattern the converter already uses for code spans and bold/italic text (protect the span from escaping via the tokenizer's `store()` mechanism, restore verbatim after all other substitutions). Verified by rendering the actual compiled page, not just checking the `.tex` source: the math renders correctly as italic subscripted variables.

**The finding was moved into the Abstract, trimming an existing parenthetical to control net growth**, per this project's standing awareness (since the seventh review) that the abstract is already dense. The notice-retrievability sentence now contrasts directly against the replacement-evidence numbers (60.0% recall@10, 62.7% citation-given-retrieval) in the same breath, and the GPT-OSS sample-size parenthetical was shortened to make room, rather than simply appending and letting the abstract grow further.

**Three items were re-affirmed as declined, unchanged from ADR-163, since nothing about this review changed the underlying resource/credential facts**: a matched non-retracted control set (new annotation labor), a placebo-retrieval condition (blocked on the same missing `GROQ_API_KEY` and unpulled Ollama models ADR-163 already checked), and a second annotator (real human judgment this session cannot manufacture). Re-verifying these was not repeated, since ADR-163's own checks (env inspection, Ollama API probe) are still valid -- nothing in this session's environment changed between rounds.

**Two items were deferred to the author's own explicit decision, not executed unilaterally:**
- **Figure 1 redesign.** The review's proposed extended chain (superseded source -> correction discovered -> current evidence retrieved -> superseded source avoided -> current evidence used -> correct answer) is a real structural change to a figure that has been revised, cross-reference-audited, and visually re-verified across roughly six prior rounds (ADR-153 through ADR-162 collectively). The replacement-retrieval finding is now prominently reported in prose (Abstract, Results, Discussion) rather than left undiscovered; redesigning the paper's most load-bearing figure carries real risk of introducing a new visual or cross-reference bug for a presentational gain prose already delivers, and was judged not worth attempting without the author confirming they want that specific risk taken.
- **Retitling the paper** around "Supersession-Aware Groundedness." A title change affects the paper's citable identity going forward and is explicitly the kind of framing decision this project's own standing practice (most recently the strategic fork surfaced at the end of ADR-161) treats as the author's call, not something to execute inside a text-fixing pass.

**Verified, not assumed:** `scripts/replacement_citation_check.py` run once, producing the numbers reported above; the response-id overlap between this check's "replacement retrieved" set and the Correctness hand-labeled sample was computed directly via set intersection (12), not estimated. `paper/latex/build.py`'s new math-mode handling verified by rebuilding `main.tex` and grepping the output for `$R_c$` etc. before compiling, then rendering the compiled page to PNG and reading it directly to confirm proper subscripted-italic math rendering, not just clean LaTeX source. `uv run retraction-bench evaluate` re-run from a clean deletion -- unchanged, no core-pipeline computation touched. `python -m pytest tests/`: 358 passed. `pdflatex`/`bibtex`/`pdflatex`x2 compiled clean -- 0 errors, 0 undefined references, confirmed against a freshly-timestamped `main.pdf`. Citation/bibliography check re-run, still a perfect 22/22 match. Page count unchanged at 37 (new content balanced against the trimmed abstract parenthetical and no new subsection numbering). `scripts/replacement_retrieval_ceiling.py` was also re-run this round as a determinism check on already-committed output; byte-identical, confirmed via `git diff` showing no changes to the committed JSON.

**Consequences:** `paper/paper.md` revised in the Abstract, section 4.5 (new paragraph), Discussion (new notation paragraph), Limitations (one bullet rewritten again), and Future Work (one bullet rewritten again). `paper/latex/build.py` gained inline-math support (one new regex substitution, documented inline). New files: `scripts/replacement_citation_check.py`, `results/replacement_citation_check.json`, `results/replacement_citation_check.md`. Figure 1, the matched-control gap, the placebo-condition gap, the second-annotator gap, and the paper's title all remain open, explicitly deferred to the author -- the first three for checked resource/credential reasons, the last two because they are framing decisions this project's standing practice reserves for the author, not editorial fixes to execute inside a revision pass.

## ADR-165: Eleventh external review acted on -- a fabricated citation recurs and is re-rejected; the 62.7% replacement-citation statistic corrected to item-clustered; the five-variable notation's mixed observational units made explicit; Related Work narrowed again with one more verified citation; the venue-strategy question surfaced directly rather than deferred a fourth time

**Status:** Accepted

**Context:** An eleventh external review, framed explicitly as "would I let my student submit this as their flagship paper," judged the manuscript strong-but-not-yet-exceptional and again cited the "Trojan Paper Medical Benchmark" (SSRN abstract_id=7018684) as evidence the paper's novelty claims need tightening -- this time with a specific-looking SSRN delivery URL rather than a bare abstract link. It also raised two new, real, checkable statistical/notational precision issues and cited six other external sources in support of a venue-strategy argument.

**Every citation was independently verified before any paper change was made, continuing the discipline ADR-161 established.** Six of seven checked out real, with exact details matching the review's characterization: Yadav (2026, arXiv:2606.26511, MemStrata), the JAMIA sleep-medicine RAG paper (Li et al., DOI 10.1093/jamia/ocag056), npj Digital Medicine's own aims-and-scope statement (confirmed verbatim against Nature's page), the SC-RAG ScienceDirect paper (S0306457325003103), Research Integrity and Peer Review's submission guidelines, and the SN Computer Science RAG-evaluation review (10.1007/s42979-026-05134-x). **The Trojan Paper Medical Benchmark did not verify, for the second time.** A direct `WebFetch` against the cited SSRN delivery URL returned HTTP 403 (SSRN blocks automated fetches, an expected and inconclusive result on its own), but combined with zero search-engine trace of the title, the named authors, or the abstract ID across two independent rounds of checking, and ADR-161's own earlier direct confirmation that "abstract ID 7018684 does not appear to exist in the SSRN database," this citation is treated as unverified and is not cited anywhere in this paper. This is disclosed prominently, not quietly: a citation recurring a second time, dressed with a more specific-looking URL, is exactly the pattern that should increase scrutiny, not credibility.

**Two real, correct precision issues were identified and fixed, both actionable without new experiments:**
- **The 62.7% replacement-citation-given-retrieval statistic (added in ADR-164) was a naive per-response rate, not item-clustered**, despite the 126 "retrieved" responses being repeated generations over only 7 items (retrieval is fixed per item; only model/seed/condition vary). This is exactly the pseudoreplication error ADR-151 fixed project-wide for M1 in an earlier round -- missed here because the statistic was new, not because the discipline was abandoned. Fixed by extending `scripts/replacement_citation_check.py` to call `stats/bootstrap.py`'s existing `bootstrap_proportion_ci_clustered` (the same function used everywhere else in this paper), clustering on `item_id`. Result: 95% CI [37.3%, 85.7%] -- genuinely wide, an honest consequence of only 7 clusters, not a bug. Updated in the Abstract, section 4.5, and the script's own markdown output.
- **The five-variable notation ($R_c, R_r, U_s, U_r, C$, added in ADR-164) risked implying a jointly-observed five-state vector on one observational unit, when the underlying measurements actually live at two different levels** ($R_c$/$R_r$ are item-level, fixed by query and corpus; $U_s$/$U_r$ are response-level, varying by model/seed/condition; $C$ is response-level but recorded only on a separate 100-response hand-labeled subsample). The Discussion paragraph introducing this notation was rewritten to state this explicitly, reframed from "five binary outcomes forming a unified state" to "five operational measurements spanning the evidence lifecycle," with the one legitimately joint state ($R_c=1, R_r=1, U_s=0, U_r=0$, instantiated by RB-0018) reframed as observed at the level where it actually was jointly observed -- a single item's own responses -- not implied to be a general per-response joint distribution.

**The literature-gap language was narrowed a further step, using the one new verified citation (Yadav 2026).** The "Temporal validity and knowledge conflict" paragraph in Related Work now explicitly states that stale-versus-current retrieval is established as a broader structural RAG problem in its own right (not only a retracted-literature-recognition problem), and repositions RetractionBench's contribution accordingly: not the observation that either problem exists, but a medical evidence-lifecycle benchmark separately measuring correction retrieval, replacement retrieval, superseded-source citation, replacement-source citation, and substantive correctness under one common, hand-verified benchmark -- matching the review's own suggested safer framing near-verbatim, since it was independently judged correct on its merits, not merely conceded.

**The "downstream evidence-use failure" primary/secondary framing was applied to the one Discussion sentence that still led with "evidence-integration bottleneck" unhedged** (the section 5 opening sentence; every other occurrence already said "consistent with," per ADR-160/161's own prior fixes, and was left unchanged).

**Everything else in this review -- the matched-control, placebo-condition, correctness-linkage, and second-annotator requests, the "Trojan-style benchmark" distinguishing sentence, and the venue-identity/title reframing -- was not actioned, and for the first time this project's response explicitly names why rather than only restating the same resource-gated conclusion a fourth time.** The first four require new experiments or human labor this session still cannot manufacture (unchanged since ADR-163's own credential/infrastructure check, re-confirmed not to have changed). The "Trojan-style" distinguishing sentence was not written because the paper it would distinguish against cannot be verified to exist. The venue/title reframing is a real, well-supported strategic argument (six of its seven supporting citations are genuine, and the venue characterizations check out) but is exactly the kind of decision -- affecting the paper's citable identity and requiring the author's own judgment about available time and priorities -- that this project's standing practice reserves for the author (most recently stated explicitly at the end of ADR-161). Three consecutive external-review rounds (ADR-163, ADR-164, this one) have now converged on substantially the same four resource-gated asks; this ADR's own closing action is to surface that convergence to the author directly as a decision to make, rather than deferring it silently a fourth time.

**Verified, not assumed:** citation-existence checks for all seven sources in this review (six confirmed real via multiple independent search results with matching authors/venues/DOIs; one confirmed still-unverifiable via a direct `WebFetch` attempt plus repeated search-engine silence). `scripts/replacement_citation_check.py` re-run, producing the item-clustered CI reported above; its point estimate (0.627) is unchanged from ADR-164's naive figure, confirming the fix changed only the uncertainty estimate, not the underlying data. `uv run retraction-bench evaluate` re-run from a clean deletion -- unchanged, no core-pipeline computation touched. `python -m pytest tests/`: 358 passed. `paper/latex/build.py` regenerated `main.tex`; `pdflatex`/`bibtex`/`pdflatex`x2 compiled clean -- 0 errors, 0 undefined references, confirmed against a freshly-timestamped `main.pdf`. Citation/bibliography consistency check re-run and expanded to a perfect 23/23 match (up from 22/22, the one new Yadav entry). Page count: 37 -> 38 (one page, from the new Related Work paragraph, the item-clustered CI explanation, and the notation paragraph's expansion). The item-clustered-CI page, the notation-precision page, and the Related Work page all rendered to PNG and read directly.

**Consequences:** `paper/paper.md` revised in Related Work (one paragraph rewritten, one new citation), the Abstract (item-clustered CI added), section 4.5 (item-clustered CI added), and Discussion (two paragraphs revised: the notation paragraph and the opening sentence). `scripts/replacement_citation_check.py` gained item-clustered CI computation. `paper/latex/refs.bib` and `build.py`'s `CITE_MAP` each gained one entry (Yadav 2026). This is the second round in this paper's revision history where a cited external paper failed verification (after ADR-161's Sebo & Sebo finding), and the first time the same fabricated-or-unfindable citation has recurred across separate review rounds -- worth remembering if it appears a third time. The four resource-gated items and the venue/title question remain explicitly open, named in this ADR as a decision point for the author rather than restated as a fourth silent deferral.

## ADR-166: Manuscript finalized at 38 pages -- the author's explicit decision, closing the external-review revision series

**Status:** Accepted

**Context:** ADR-165 closed by naming, directly, that three consecutive external-review rounds (ADR-163, ADR-164, ADR-165) had converged on the same four resource-gated requests (matched non-retracted controls, a placebo-retrieval condition, correctness-linkage annotation joining replacement citation to hand-labeled Correctness, and a second independent annotator) and surfaced this to the author as an explicit decision rather than continuing to defer it silently. Presented with the choice, the author chose to finalize the manuscript as it stands rather than invest further session time or external resources into the four items above.

**This ADR records that decision as the closing entry of the review-response series that began with ADR-153.** It does not itself change `paper.md`, any figure, any script, or any reported number -- the manuscript at the end of ADR-165's commit (`91f05cb`) is the final state. Re-running the full verification suite confirmed nothing has drifted since that commit: `python -m pytest tests/` (358 passed), `uv run retraction-bench evaluate` (unchanged from a clean deletion), a fresh `pdflatex`/`bibtex`/`pdflatex`x2 compile (0 errors, 0 undefined references, 38 pages), and the citation/bibliography consistency check (23/23, unchanged).

**What "finalized" means here, precisely, so a future reader does not mistake silence for an oversight:**
- The four resource-gated items (matched controls, placebo condition, correctness-linkage annotation, second annotator) remain named explicitly in Limitations and Future Work, exactly as they have been since their respective rounds -- they are not hidden, softened, or silently dropped by this decision. They are disclosed as open follow-on work requiring resources this project's zero-cost, single-annotator constraints do not provide, a limitation the manuscript itself states plainly rather than apologizes for.
- The paper's title and its "supersession-aware evidence grounding" reframing (proposed across the ninth through eleventh reviews) were not adopted. The manuscript keeps its existing title and its existing three-contribution framing (a hand-verified benchmark; an evaluation framework separating retrieval, citation avoidance, replacement retrieval/citation, and correctness; empirical evidence that these do not move together), established in ADR-157 and refined through ADR-165.
- Figure 1 was not redesigned. The evidence-lifecycle chain it depicts, and the newer replacement-retrieval/citation findings, are integrated in prose (Abstract, section 4.5, Discussion) rather than in a rebuilt figure, per the risk/reward judgment recorded in ADR-164 and reaffirmed in ADR-165.
- The unverifiable "Trojan Paper Medical Benchmark" citation, encountered in both ADR-161 and ADR-165, remains uncited. If it resurfaces in any future review of this manuscript, the same verification result should be expected unless independently re-checked and found otherwise -- it should not be assumed correct merely because it has appeared multiple times.

**What the eleven external-review rounds (ADR-153 through ADR-165) collectively changed, for the record:** a full reproducibility gap closed with committed, tested code (ADR-153); a page-count reduction from 48 to a low of 33 pages across three condensation passes, then deliberate, disclosed increases to 38 as later rounds added a Conclusion section, causal-language precision fixes, a Related Work expansion grounded in independently-verified contemporaneous literature, and two new empirical measurements (replacement-evidence retrieval and generation-side replacement citation) built entirely from infrastructure and cached data this project already owned; two real LaTeX/cross-reference bugs and one real bibliography-wiring bug found and fixed by systematic, not incidental, verification; two attempted citations (Sebo & Sebo; the Trojan Paper Medical Benchmark) checked and rejected before being cited, rather than trusted because a reviewer supplied them; and a running, append-only decision log (this file) documenting every one of those changes with what was verified and how.

**Consequences:** No files changed by this ADR beyond `docs/DECISIONS.md` itself. `FINAL_REPRODUCIBILITY_REPORT.md`, `FINAL_REVIEW.md`, and the project memory file are updated alongside this entry to state the same finalization decision. Any future work on this manuscript -- a new external review, a venue-specific formatting pass, or a decision to pursue the four resource-gated items after all -- should be treated as a new phase, referencing this ADR as the point at which the author closed the prior one, not as a continuation of an open-ended revision loop.

## ADR-167: Author-directed reframing -- Figure 1 rebuilt around all five measurements ($R_c, R_r, U_s, U_r, C$), the Introduction/Conclusion updated to match, the Abstract's 62.7% denominator spelled out explicitly

**Status:** Accepted

**Context:** ADR-166 closed the external-review revision series at the author's request. Immediately afterward, the author reopened work directly -- not via another pasted external review, but with seven explicit, self-authored editorial instructions: update the central framing (Introduction and Figure 1) to match the five measurements the Results and Discussion already report; rewrite the Conclusion to reflect the same five-measurement story and lead with "finding the correction is not equivalent to finding the current evidence, and finding the current evidence is not equivalent to using it"; make the Abstract's 62.7% figure state its exact denominator (126, of which 79) rather than a bare percentage; confirm the "evidence-integration bottleneck" language stays properly hedged; keep the replacement-retrieval result clearly exploratory; run a full consistency audit for stale three-outcome language; and keep the whole pass short. This ADR is that pass, executed directly since these are the author's own explicit instructions, not claims requiring independent verification.

**Figure 1 was redesigned for the first time since ADR-153 -- the one change every prior round (ADR-160, ADR-164, ADR-165) had deliberately declined as too risky without the author's own explicit direction.** The bottom chain, previously three boxes (retrieved / citation avoided / correct), is now five boxes matching the Discussion's own notation exactly, box for variable: **Correction RETRIEVED ($R_c$) → Replacement RETRIEVED ($R_r$) → Trap source AVOIDED ($U_s$) → Replacement CITED ($U_r$) → Answer CORRECT ($C$)**, each labeled with its own section reference. Arrows were changed from the prior version's red "does NOT imply" callouts (appropriate for three transitions, visually excessive for four) to neutral gray arrows plus a single italic caveat stating precisely what the ninth review's statistical critique required: $R_c$/$R_r$ are item-level, $U_s$/$U_r$/$C$ are response-level with $C$ on a separate hand-labeled subsample, and the chain is not a jointly observed or proven causal sequence. A second line of real numbers (notice vs. replacement recall, the 62.7% citation-given-retrieval rate with its item-clustered CI, the per-model citation-avoidance split) grounds the schematic in the paper's own reported findings rather than leaving it purely conceptual. Building this required matplotlib's built-in mathtext support for the `$R_c$`-style labels (confirmed to render correctly without needing full LaTeX math mode, since these are simple subscripted variables) and one real spacing bug caught by rendering, not assumed correct from the code: the italic caveat text's first line initially overlapped the box borders at the original `y=0.45` placement; fixed by moving the box block up (`BY` from 0.75 to 1.05) and the caveat text down (`y` from 0.45 to 0.60, with explicit `va="top"` alignment), re-rendered, and re-verified visually.

**The Introduction's three-contributions paragraph and Figure 1's own caption were updated to match**: contribution two now names all five measurements explicitly ("whether the correction is retrieved, whether the replacement evidence is retrieved, whether the superseded source is avoided, whether the replacement source is cited, and whether the resulting answer is substantively correct -- five separate measurements with their own denominators, not a proven causal chain"), and contribution three's summary was extended to state that correction retrieval succeeds far more reliably than replacement retrieval, not only that citation avoidance is heterogeneous. "This paper makes **three** contributions" was deliberately left unchanged -- that is a true, still-accurate claim about the paper's contribution *structure* (benchmark, framework, empirical evidence), not a restatement of the outcome count the reframing was about, and the author's own instruction was explicit that statements like this should remain where they describe something genuinely still three-part.

**The Conclusion was rewritten to lead with the requested framing.** Paragraph one's "three measurable outcomes" became the same five-measurement list Figure 1 and the Introduction now use. Paragraph two gained the replacement-retrieval/citation asymmetry (previously present in Results and Discussion but absent from the Conclusion's own summary). Paragraph three's closing sentence was rewritten to state, in the author's own requested words, that "finding the correction is not equivalent to finding the current evidence, and finding the current evidence is not equivalent to using it," with the original retrieval/citation-avoidance/correctness sentence retained immediately after as a second, still-true instance of the same pattern rather than replaced by it -- the fuller chain subsumes the narrower one, it does not contradict it.

**The Abstract's 62.7% figure was rewritten to state its exact numerator and denominator**, per the author's own suggested wording almost verbatim: "Among the 126 responses in which replacement evidence was actually present in the retrieved context (not all responses), 79 (62.7%) cited it," retaining the item-clustered CI and cluster count already added in ADR-165. This removes any remaining risk that a reader skims past the parenthetical CI note and reads 62.7% as computed over all 900 (or even all 234) responses.

**A full consistency audit was run, not assumed complete from memory of what was changed.** Grepped the entire manuscript for "three measurable outcomes" (0 remaining, previously 3), "three outcomes" (0 remaining), "retrieval, citation avoidance, and correctness" as a totalizing phrase (0 remaining), "three contributions" (2 occurrences, both correctly describing the three-part contribution structure, left unchanged per the author's own stated exception), and every "Figure 1" cross-reference (4 occurrences, 2 already correct, 2 fixed: the Related Work's Wallat-et-al. paragraph, which described the paper's distinction as if it were only the three-part retrieved/avoided/correct axis, now calls that "one thread of Figure 1's fuller five-measurement chain"; and the item-walkthrough figure's own caption, which claimed to show "exactly the retrieved != citation avoided != correct chain in Figure 1," now states the specific measured values for that one item, $R_c=1, U_s=1, C=0$, rather than implying it represents Figure 1's entire chain). **A third, matching stale reference was found and fixed inside the item-walkthrough figure's own embedded image** (not just its LaTeX caption): `make_item_walkthrough.py`'s scored-outcome annotation still read "retrieved != used != correct," using "used" -- the exact imprecise term ADR-157 replaced with "citation avoided" everywhere else in the manuscript over a year of prior revision rounds, missed in this one script because it was never touched by that round's own audit. Fixed to "correction retrieved != trap source avoided != answer correct," regenerated, and re-verified by rendering.

**Length was kept in check, not padded.** Net change: `paper.md` 1759 -> 1768 lines (+9), almost entirely the Conclusion rewrite the author explicitly requested; every other edit either replaced existing text in place or trimmed alongside adding (e.g., the Related Work sentence was shortened, not merely appended to). Final PDF page count is unchanged at 38.

**A transient, unrelated infrastructure issue was encountered and resolved during this round, worth recording since it could recur:** `scripts/make_paper_figures.py`'s first run this round failed partway through with `MemoryError: bad allocation` inside `make_forest_plot.py`'s PNG save step. Checked directly rather than assumed to be a code bug: `Get-CimInstance Win32_OperatingSystem` showed under 1.4GB of the machine's 16GB free at the time, consistent with several concurrent IDE/browser processes (VS Code, Chrome) holding most of it. Re-running the same script minutes later succeeded without any code change, confirming this was transient system memory pressure, not a regression in `make_forest_plot.py` -- no fix was needed or made to that script.

**Verified, not assumed:** `scripts/make_paper_figures.py` re-run in full after the transient failure resolved -- all 9 figures regenerated with 0 errors. `paper/latex/build.py` regenerated `main.tex`; `pdflatex`/`bibtex`/`pdflatex`x2 compiled clean -- 0 errors, 0 undefined references, confirmed against a freshly-timestamped `main.pdf`, 38 pages. Citation/bibliography consistency check re-run, unchanged at 23/23 (no citations touched this round). `uv run retraction-bench evaluate` re-run from a clean deletion -- unchanged, no core-pipeline computation touched. `python -m pytest tests/`: 358 passed. The redesigned Figure 1 (both its standalone PNG and its rendering inside the compiled PDF), the Conclusion page, the Abstract page, and the corrected item-walkthrough figure were all rendered to PNG and read directly, not assumed correct from source inspection alone -- this is how the box/caveat-text overlap bug and the item-walkthrough script's stale "used" wording were actually caught.

**Consequences:** `paper/paper.md` revised in the Abstract (denominator spelled out), Introduction (three-contributions paragraph, Figure 1's caption), Related Work (the Wallat-et-al. paragraph's Figure 1 reference), Results section 4.2 (the item-walkthrough figure's own LaTeX caption), and the Conclusion (rewritten). `paper/latex/figures/make_conceptual_figure.py` substantially rewritten (five-box chain, new spacing, new grounding-numbers text) and `make_item_walkthrough.py` had one stale line corrected. This is the first round in the manuscript's revision history driven by the author's own direct editorial instructions rather than a review to evaluate and selectively act on -- executed without the citation-verification or resource-feasibility gating those rounds required, since the author is not an external source whose claims need independent checking.

## ADR-168: Visual/figure redesign pass -- one semantic color system, Figure 1 rebuilt as a single horizontal chain, Figure 4 (redundant M1 bar chart) removed, Figures 7 and 9 extended/redesigned

**Status:** Accepted

**Context:** The author requested a visual-only redesign of every figure toward a restrained, Q1-biomedical-journal aesthetic -- no new experiments, no changed numbers, no rewritten scientific argument. Added `paper/latex/figures/_style.py`, a shared module defining one semantic palette (red = superseded/retracted, amber = correction/notice, blue = retrieval/system, green = current/authoritative, gray = distractor/background) plus common rcParams, imported by every figure script for consistency.

**Figure 1** rebuilt from ADR-167's two-panel, two-title, two-paragraph version into ONE horizontal nine-box chain (original study -> superseded -> correction notice -> $R_c$ -> replacement evidence -> $R_r$ -> $U_s$ -> $U_r$ -> $C$), content boxes colored by evidence semantics and measurement boxes colored blue/green with bold variable names. All explanatory prose (denominator warnings, item- vs. response-level distinction, grounding numbers) moved out of the graphic into the caption, per the brief's "no paragraph-sized text in the figure" rule.

**Figure 4** (M1-by-model-and-condition bar chart) removed from the manuscript as redundant with the by-model-x-condition table immediately above it and with the forest plot (Figure 5, now renumbered Figure 4) immediately below. Since figures are numbered automatically by LaTeX from sequential placement, not `\ref`/`\label`, removing one figure shifted every later figure's number by one; the two hardcoded textual references ("also shown as Figure 5" -> "Figure 4"; "see also Figure 9" -> "Figure 8") were found by grep and updated. `make_figures.py` still generates the unused PDF (harmless, not embedded) rather than deleting the code.

**Figure 5 (forest plot)** had its y-axis tick labels stripped of embedded editorial interpretation ("no observed change" / "real decrease" / "inconclusive") -- now model names only, per the brief's "no editorial labels in the axis itself, use neutral statistical language" rule; the numeric CI/Holm-p annotations (already neutral) were kept.

**Figure 8 (failure-cascade / executive-summary chart)** extended from six to eight measurements, adding "replacement ranks in top 10" (60.0%, from `results/replacement_retrieval_ceiling.json`) and "replacement cited, given retrieved" (62.7%, from `results/replacement_citation_check.json`) to the retrieval/citation-behavior block -- both loaded from their existing result JSONs, not transcribed by hand, matching the script's own stated discipline for every other bar. Recolored to the shared semantic palette (blue = retrieval, red = citing superseded evidence, green = citing/matching current evidence, amber = correction-related, gray = record infrastructure).

**Figure 7 (M3)** converted from a hatched grouped-bar-with-error-whiskers chart to a 100%-stacked horizontal proportion chart, one bar per model, segments colored acknowledges=green/silently_revises=amber/defends=red (a natural fit to the semantic system: defending the retracted claim = red, aligning with the correction = green). gpt-oss-120b's n=6 is de-emphasized by reduced fill opacity and an explicit "(n=6, descriptive only)" row label rather than hatching, per the brief's "no hatching unless genuinely necessary" rule. Per-category 95% CIs, which don't render as on-bar whiskers on a stacked chart without visually colliding with the adjacent segment, are kept as compact text beside each row instead of being dropped.

**Figures 2, 3, 6, 8 (retrievability panels)** recolored to the shared semantic palette without structural changes; Figure 2's funnel counts enlarged and made the dominant visual element (17pt bold numbers above each stage box, small text below) per the brief's "make counts visually dominant" instruction.

**Verified, not assumed:** every figure script re-run via `scripts/make_paper_figures.py` (0 errors this time; the ADR-167 round's transient `MemoryError` did not recur). `paper/latex/build.py` regenerated `main.tex`; `pdflatex`/`bibtex`/`pdflatex`x2 compiled clean -- 0 errors, 0 undefined references, 38 pages (unchanged). Bibliography check clean (23/23, no citations touched). `python -m pytest tests/`: 358 passed. Every changed figure was rendered to PNG (both standalone and inside the compiled PDF) and read directly -- this is how a suspected box-clipping issue in Figure 3 was checked and ruled a false alarm (a pixel-level check confirmed real white margin at the figure edge; the "unrounded" corners on taller boxes are a real but cosmetically minor artifact of a fixed rounding radius on a taller box, not a bug, and were left as-is). Final grep for stale three-outcome language ("three measurable outcomes," "three outcomes," "Figure 9," "Figure 5)") returned zero matches.

**Consequences:** New file `paper/latex/figures/_style.py`. Rewrote `make_conceptual_figure.py` (Figure 1, full redesign), `make_schematics.py` (Figures 2-3, recolor + funnel count emphasis), `make_figures.py` (Figure 7 restructured, Figure 4's chart still generated but unreferenced), `make_forest_plot.py` (Figure 5, axis label cleanup), `make_failure_cascade.py` (Figure 8, extended to eight bars), `make_item_walkthrough.py` and `make_retrievability_panels.py` (recolor only). `paper/paper.md` edited: Figure 1's caption rewritten, the Figure-4 bar chart and its embed removed (replaced with one transitional sentence), two hardcoded figure-number references updated, Figure 8's caption updated for the new bar count.

## ADR-169: Final visual-polish pass -- Figure 1 enlarged and item/response-level tags added, Figure 2's stages grouped, Figure 4's model color-coding removed, Figure 5 rebuilt as a compact case-study schematic, Figure 7 rebuilt around a "24/24" callout, Figure 8 split into three real panels

**Status:** Accepted

**Context:** A follow-up visual-only brief, more specific than ADR-168's first pass: enlarge Figure 1 (the paper's visual identity) by ~25-35% and make it self-explanatory in ~5 seconds; distinguish Figure 2's heuristic-prefilter vs. human-verification stages visually, not just by label; remove all per-model color-coding everywhere (model identity must read as neutral, strong colors reserved for evidence semantics) and use short publication-style model names (Llama 3.1 8B / GPT-OSS 120B / Qwen 2.5 7B); rebuild the item-walkthrough figure as a compact context-cards -> model-output -> outcome schematic instead of five stacked paragraph boxes; make Figure 7's headline result (24/24 notices rank top-3) immediately countable rather than statistically implied; and split Figure 8 into three visually separate panels instead of one bar axis with a dashed divider. Plus one leftover wording fix: Related Work still said the pipeline measures "three separate...outcomes."

**Figure 1**: `build.py` now special-cases `fig:conceptual` to `0.98\textwidth` (vs. every other figure's `0.92\textwidth`), and the figure itself grew taller with larger type (box height 1.75->2.35, base fontsize ~7.6->9.6, title 12->15pt) -- combined, roughly the requested 25-35% more page area. Each of the five measurement boxes ($R_c, R_r, U_s, U_r, C$) now carries a small "item-level" / "per response" tag directly in the graphic, not just in the caption, per the brief's explicit request to make that distinction visible in the figure itself, not caption-only.

**Figure 2**: the funnel's four stages now carry a bold-text group label underneath ("Raw source" / "Heuristic pre-filters (recall-oriented)" / "Human verification"), and the final "Eligibility verification" box gets a heavier 2pt border to mark it as the actual benchmark, not just another pre-filter stage. **A real bug was caught and fixed here by full-page rendering, not just standalone-script rendering**: an intermediate version placed the group-label text too close to the box's bottom edge, and while the standalone cropped PNG looked fine, the same PDF embedded at print scale on the compiled page showed the text visually colliding with the box border -- caught only by rendering the actual compiled PDF page at high resolution and reading it directly, not by trusting the standalone figure check. Fixed by increasing the label's vertical offset from the box (0.9-0.28 -> 0.9-0.42) and re-verified on the rebuilt page.

**Figure 4 (forest plot)**: `_style.py`'s `MODEL_COLOR` per-model mapping (blue/gray/red by model) was removed entirely and replaced with `MODEL_NEUTRAL` (one blue for every model's point/line), since a model-specific color in a forest plot risks reading as a ranking cue even without an explicit claim. Model labels shortened to publication style. The effect-estimate/CI text and the Holm-p text were split into two visually distinct lines (numbers, then a smaller gray p-value line) instead of one run-on string.

**Figure 5 (case study, formerly a five-stacked-paragraph-box layout)**: rebuilt as context cards (trap source / notice / distractors / current evidence, left) -> model output (center, verbatim quote shortened with an ellipsis, no words changed) -> outcome tags (right: "CITED (not avoided)" / "INCORRECT") -> a compact gold-answer comparison strip -> a bottom row of three outlined badges ($R_c{=}1$, $U_s{=}1$, $C{=}0$) with a note that $R_r$/$U_r$ are not measured for this item, per the brief's "only include Rr/Ur if actually measured for this example." No new text invented; every quoted fragment is the same verbatim source already in paper.md section 4.2, just visually shortened.

**Figure 7 (retrieval rank)**: Panel A replaced jittered scatter with a deterministic stacked-dot display (dots sharing a rank stack in a column at that rank, no randomness), plus a large "24/24 / rank <= 3" callout drawn directly in the panel's own empty space. The figure's own caption in `paper.md` was corrected to match -- it previously said panel A was "jittered for visibility," which stopped being true once the jitter was replaced by exact stacking; left uncorrected this would have been a new figure/caption mismatch of exactly the kind this project's QA passes exist to catch. Panel B's domain coloring was kept but muted (smaller markers, lower alpha, gray-toned legend text) per "keep domain information subtle."

**Figure 8 (executive summary)**: converted from one bar axis with a dashed divider line to three genuinely separate matplotlib panels (`gridspec width_ratios`, real whitespace between them, each with its own light background tint), with a small gray "›" chevron glyph in each gap marking a conceptual, non-causal separator. Building this required two rounds of width/font tuning after the first attempt produced overlapping tick labels in the 5-bar and 2-bar panels (`tick_fs` set narrower for panels with >=4 bars; panel width ratios rebalanced to `[1.1, 5.3, 2.5]`; two labels shortened) -- caught by rendering, not assumed correct from the code.

**Wording fix**: Related Work's "retrieval availability, citation avoidance, and substantive correctness can be measured as three separate...outcomes" rewritten to name all five measurements explicitly, matching the framework everywhere else in the manuscript.

**Verified, not assumed**: full `scripts/make_paper_figures.py` regeneration (9/9 clean), `paper/latex/build.py` + `pdflatex`/`bibtex`/`pdflatex`x2 (0 errors, 0 undefined refs, 38 pages, bibliography 23/23 unchanged), `python -m pytest tests/` (358 passed), every changed page (3, 8, 35-38) rendered to PNG and read directly at print resolution -- this is what caught the Figure 2 label/border collision that the standalone-script check alone had missed, and confirmed no clipping, no overlapping text, and no stray jitter language survived into the final captions.

**Remaining, disclosed, not fixed**: pages 37 and 38 each carry a large blank margin above their figure (Figures 7 and 8), a standard LaTeX float-placement artifact of a `[ht]` figure landing with little preceding text on its page, not a defect introduced by this round's edits. Fixing it would mean tuning float placement (or forcing `[H]`/inserting `\FloatBarrier`s) globally across a 38-page document this late in the revision history, which risks shifting pagination elsewhere; left as a known, reported issue rather than risked.

**Consequences:** `paper/latex/build.py` (per-figure width override), `paper/latex/figures/_style.py` (`MODEL_COLOR` -> `MODEL_NEUTRAL`), `make_conceptual_figure.py`, `make_schematics.py`, `make_forest_plot.py`, `make_item_walkthrough.py` (full rewrite), `make_retrievability_panels.py`, `make_failure_cascade.py`, `make_figures.py` (M3 model labels) all edited. `paper/paper.md`: one Related Work sentence, Figure 7's caption.
